- `xdr_SDBfileHeader` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the XDR codec of the database file header, a 320-byte routine at 0x10024470. Established.
  It calls `strlen` on its second argument (the header record), codes four bytes at record offset 0 with `xdr_bytes`, the two version numbers at +4 and +8 with `xdr_SDBversionNumber`, a 100-byte byte array at +12, the element count at +112 with `xdr_SDBelementIndex`, two file offsets at +116 and +120 with `xdr_SDBfileOffset`, and ten words at +124 with `xdr_array` (`xdr_u_long`, element size 4). Any failing step returns 0; success returns 1.
- `xdr_SDBversionNumber` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the XDR codec of a two-halfword version number, a 104-byte routine at 0x1002471c. Established.
  It codes the halfword at offset 0 and then the halfword at offset 2 with `xdr_u_short`, returning 0 at the first failure and 1 when both succeed.
- `xdr_SDBelementIndex` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the XDR codec of an element index, a 52-byte routine at 0x100242b4. Established.
  It tail-calls `xdr_u_long` with its own arguments.
- `xdr_SDBfileOffset` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the XDR codec of a file offset, a 52-byte routine at 0x100242e8. Established.
  It tail-calls `xdr_u_long` with its own arguments.
- `ng1_setmon` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: loads a monitor timing file for the older board family that `setmon` also supports and applies it through the X video extension, a 1676-byte routine at 0x1000f064. Established.
  The arguments are the graphics device descriptor (a0, kept in s1 until the video-format step), a board record (a1, s7), the timing file name (a2, s5) and a verbose flag (a4, s2); a3 is not read. It returns 0 on success and -1 when the file name is too long or the file cannot be opened. Every other failure calls `exit` with 1.
  File name. When the byte at record `+40` is below 3 the name is used as given. Otherwise the suffix is `_r3.u` when that byte is 3, or when it is 4 or more and the byte at record `+64` is below 11, and `_r4.u` when it is 4 or more and the byte at `+64` is 11 or more (the strings at 0x10042108 and 0x10042100, chosen at 0x1000f0c0 to 0x1000f0d4). If `strstr` finds the suffix in the name it is used as it is. If not, and `strlen` gives 90 or less, it clears a buffer of that length on the stack, copies the name minus its last two characters with `strncpy` and appends the suffix with `strcat`; a longer name prints "setmon:filename %s too long" and returns -1. With the verbose flag set it prints "ng1_setmon: Loading monitor timing from %s" with `printf`.
  Timing file. It opens the name with `fopen` mode `r`; on failure it prints "setmon: Couldn't find file %s" with `printf`, logs the name with `syslog` at priority 2 and returns -1. It reads one line of up to 4095 bytes with `fgets`, removes a newline found with `strchr`, and parses `%x,%x,%x` with `sscanf` into three stack words, then stores the first as a word at 4216(sp) and the second and third as halfwords at 4220(sp) and 4222(sp) (0x1000f340, 0x1000f348, 0x1000f354). When `fgets` returns nothing those three slots are not written and keep whatever the stack held. The file is closed with `fclose` on both paths.
  Board count. It sends request 0x65 with a zero argument through `ioctl` on the descriptor; a negative result logs "can't get number of graphics boards" with `syslog` and exits.
  Option flags in the word at 4216(sp), set from the command-line globals. When `framelockFlag` is set, the character in `framelockArg` selects: `v` (118) sets bit 3, `g` (103) sets bits 3 and 4, and any other value prints "setmon: use -F v|g, not %c" to standard error and exits. When `MSsyncFlag` is set, `MSsyncArg` of `s` (115) sets bit 11 (0x800) and `m` (109) runs the loop below, which sets bit 10 (0x400) at its end; any other value prints "setmon: use -c m|s, not %c" and exits.
  The `m` loop (0x1000f3b0 to 0x1000f590) runs for each board number from 0 up to the count, skipping the number held in `pipenum`. For each it sends request 102 with a 12-byte record of the board number, the record pointer a1 and the constant 40; a result of -1 moves on to the next board. Otherwise it sends request 104 (failure prints "graphics detach failed" and exits), then request 112 with the board number and 0x20000000 (failure prints "graphics attach failed"), request 110 (failure prints "setmon: can't map graphics"), request 0x5214 with the first record (failure prints "Couldn't set slave sync") and request 104 again (failure prints "graphics detach failed"). After the last board it sends request 112 with the number from `pipenum` and 0x20000000 (failure prints "graphics attach failed") and request 110 (failure prints "setmon: can't map graphics"). The 0x5214 request is the one [ioctls.md](ioctls.md) records at 0x1000f474.
  Video format. It takes the last `/`-separated component of the file name with `strtok` and cuts it at the first `.` found with `strchr`. It calls `XSGIvcSetPlatformParameter` with the display and screen globals (`dpy`, `screen`), parameter 0, 1, the flag word's address and length 4, then `XSGIvcLoadVideoFormatByName` with the component. A result other than 1 prints "XSGIvcLoadVideoFormatByName failed...." and exits. It then calls `XSync` with discard 1. If the byte at record `+16` is zero it returns 0. Otherwise it calls `XSGIvcSetPlatformParameter` again with parameter 0, 0, a 12-byte buffer of the flag word and the two halfwords widened to words (at 4280(sp), 4284(sp) and 4288(sp)) and length 12, calls `XSync` once more and returns 0.

# Routines of `gfxinit`, one entry each (stubs and revision fields)

Short routines of the board set-up program, read from the 6.5.30 executable `usr/gfx/gfxinit`; addresses are its virtual addresses. These belong to other boards' code that the one program carries, not to CRIME or GBE. A "direct call" means a `jal` or `j` to the routine's address.

- `venice_pgrev` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: returns 0 as one field of a board revision word, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10010310. Established.
  It returns 0 in the delay slot of its return and reads no argument. `venice_rm_rev` (0x10010360) calls it with its own argument and shifts the result left by 16 before oring it into the word it returns.
- `venice_tarev` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: returns 0 as the next field of that word, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10010320. Established.
  It returns 0 and reads no argument. `venice_rm_rev` calls it at 0x10010370 and shifts the result left by 12.
- `venice_tdrev` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: returns the constant 1 as the field below that, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10010318. Established.
  It returns 1 in the delay slot of its return and reads no argument. `venice_rm_rev` calls it at 0x10010380 and shifts the result left by 8; the same routine also takes `venice_bdrev` (shift 20), `venice_ibrev` (shift 4) and `venice_imprev` (no shift) into the word.
- `venice_gefrev` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: returns the constant 1 as the geometry-engine field of a revision word, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10010598. Established.
  It returns 1 in the delay slot of its return. `venice_ge_rev` calls it at 0x100105d4 and shifts the result left by 4, with `venice_ifrev` shifted by 8 and `venice_cprev` unshifted.
- `ev1_init` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: a board initialiser that does nothing and reports success, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10007a18. Established.
  It returns 0 in the delay slot of its return. `gr2_init` calls it at 0x10007c28 only when the byte at offset 56 of its fourth argument is positive; the call after a non-zero result (loaded through the global pointer at -32496, with 2 and a string) is therefore never reached with this routine.
- `ng1_init` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: the same do-nothing initialiser for a second board family, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10009164. Established.
  It returns 0 and touches nothing. No direct call in the executable names it.
- `fcg_jt_init` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: the scan-chain interface's initialiser, a stub, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10010778. Established.
  It returns 0 and reads no argument. No direct call in the executable names it, so any use goes through a pointer.
- `fcg_jt_hard_reset` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: the scan-chain interface's hard reset, which is not implemented, an 8-byte routine at address 0x10010818. Established.
  It returns -1 in the delay slot of its return and does nothing else, unlike `fcg_jt_soft_reset`, which is a separate 152-byte routine. No direct call names it.
- `internal_fifo_flow_control` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: an empty routine, an 8-byte routine at address 0x100120d8. Established.
  It returns at once with a `nop` in the delay slot and sets no return value. No direct call names it.
- `venice_check_vof_frame_status` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: a check that always reports ready, an 8-byte routine at address 0x100151f0. Established.
  It returns 1 in the delay slot of its return and reads no argument. No direct call names it.

# Routines of `gfxinit`, CRIME board report

The two routines that print this board's section of the board report (the program's `gfxinfo` name selects them), read in full from `objdump -d` of the 6.5.30 N32 executable `usr/gfx/gfxinit`, with the strings taken from `.rodata` and `.srdata`. The board record they walk is built by the kernel; its fields are named in [binaries.md](binaries.md#display-configuration-tools).

- `crime_monitor_info` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: prints one monitor's name from an identification block that starts with the 8-byte header 00 ff ff ff ff ff ff 00, a 216-byte routine at address 0x1000dcfc. Established.
  Its first argument is the monitor number and the second a pointer to the block. It prints "\t Monitor %d type: " (0x10022760) with the number (`printf`, call at 0x1000dd28), then calls the static routine at 0x1000dc8c with the block pointer. That routine, which `objdump` labels as part of `printXvcInfo`, returns 1 only when byte 0 is zero, bytes 1 to 6 are 0xff and byte 7 is zero (the loads at 0x1000dc8c to 0x1000dce0), and 0 on any other value.
  A result of 0 prints "Unknown" (0x10027e68; `printf` call at 0x1000ddc4). Otherwise the halfword at block `+8` is split into three letters, `((h >> 10) & 0x1f) + 64`, `((h >> 5) & 0x1f) + 64` and `(h & 0x1f) + 64` (stores at 0x1000dd60, 0x1000dd74 and 0x1000dd9c, with a zero byte after them at 0x1000dda8), and the call at 0x1000dda4 prints "%s %d" (0x10027e60) with that text and the 16-bit signed value formed from byte `+10` as the low byte and byte `+11` as the high byte. Both paths return what `printf` returns.

- `crime_info` in `usr/gfx/gfxinit`: prints the CRIME board section of the board report from the board record, a 412-byte routine at address 0x1000ddd4. Established.
  Its argument is the board record. It prints a tab (0x10027e70), then tests the byte at `+43`. A non-zero byte prints "%d + %d bitplanes\n\t" (0x10022778) with the byte at `+47` as both numbers; a zero byte prints "%d bitplanes\n\t" (0x10022790) with the byte at `+47` (the path at 0x1000df54).
  It then prints "board revision %d, " (0x100227a0) with the byte at `+40`, "CRM revision %c, " (0x100227b8) with the byte at `+41` plus 65 and "GBE revision %c\n" (0x100227d0) with the byte at `+42` plus 65. A non-zero byte at `+44` prints "\tDual Channel Display option\n" (0x100227e8).
  A non-zero word at `+56` prints "\tTiming table flags:" (0x10022808), then "  Fullscreen Stereo" (0x10022820) when bit 1 is set, "  Quadbuffer Stereo" (0x10022838) when bit 4 is set and "  Sync on green" (0x10022850) when bit 3 is set, in that order, and a newline. A zero word skips all of it.
  It then calls `crime_monitor_info` (0x1000dcfc) with 0 and the address `+100`, and, only when the byte at `+45` is non-zero, again with 1 and the address `+234`. It ends with a newline print whose result it returns. The record's bytes at `+40`, `+41`, `+42` and `+47` are only read here; their sources are in [binaries.md](binaries.md#display-configuration-tools).

# Routines of `mvp.so` (N32 build), one entry each

The N32 video library `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so` (6.5.30) keeps DWARF names, so each routine below is named by its debug-information name and addressed at its virtual address in this build; the o32 builds have other addresses and are not covered. The library reaches the video hardware only through `ioctl` on a video descriptor, with request words that start `0x6d76` (the kernel driver's `mvpioctl` splits them by their low byte, [functions.md](functions.md)). Every routine was read in full from `objdump -d`, with strings taken from `.rodata`, `.srdata` and `.sdata`, double and float constants from `.lit8` and `.lit4`, and GOT callees named by the formula PLTGOT 0x41ff18 plus 4 times local_gotno 27 plus the dynamic symbol index minus the first global symbol 37. Many of the routines share the error ladder the first of them shows. In what follows "the path" is the library's path object; its word at `+104` is the pointer that the library's own assertion text calls `path->ddPriv`, and each entry says where the descriptor for `ioctl` comes from.

- `mvpUtilityRoutine` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: classifies one request code against the path's private record, a 272-byte routine at address 0x404cf0. Established.
  The arguments are an unused first, a request code, the path and an unused fourth; only the code and the path are read. It loads the private pointer from path `+104` (0x404d1c) and, when it is zero, calls `mvpAssertFail` (0x404d44) with the text "mvph = (mvppathpriv_t *)path->ddPriv" (0x418248), the file name "mvp.c" (0x420180) and the line number 475, and carries on if that call returns.
  A code other than 100 returns 0. For code 100 the byte at private `+292` is read: zero returns 1; otherwise the byte at private `+289` is tested, 3 or 4 returns 1 and any other value returns 2.
- `mvpProxy` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: maps six small numbers onto 1 or 2, a 248-byte routine at address 0x404e00. Established.
  The argument is compared in the order 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 3: 1, 4 and 6 return 1; 2, 5 and 3 return 2; any other value is returned unchanged. The groups {1, 4, 6} and {2, 3, 5} are the ones the input-index handling of `mvpcontrol_item_set` above uses, but this routine reads no record, so reading it as that mapping is inferred from the numbers alone.
- `mvpCDev` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: maps 2 and 3 to 3, a 104-byte routine at address 0x404f00. Established.
  The argument 2 or 3 returns 3 and every other value is returned unchanged. `mvpControlIOCTL` and `mvpSendI2CData` store its result in byte `+37` of the request they send.
- `mvpPixSize` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: turns the path's format byte into a small size, a 184-byte routine at address 0x407cd0. Established.
  That the result is a pixel size in bytes is inferred from the name; the code only maps the format byte. It loads the byte at path `+295` (0x407ce0); the argument is the path. Zero, 3, 4 and 5 return 4 (the immediate is stored at 0x407d4c); 1 and 2 return 2; any other value returns the 64-bit value -1.
- `mvpCalcMitch` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: scales a width by a factor chosen from the hardware revision and the path's mode bits, a 572-byte routine at address 0x411da0. Established.
  What the factor means is not shown by the code. The arguments are the path, a width (`xsize` in the routine's own trace text) and a direction flag (`dir`). It starts from the revision, the byte at `+303` of the record whose pointer is at path `+280`, calls `mvpPixSize` (0x411dcc) with the path, and sets the factor to the float 1.0 (0x420598). It then calls `getenv` with "MVP_USEREV" (0x41a168) and, when that is not null, replaces the revision with `atoi` of the text (0x411e14).
  The path's byte at `+296` picks the factor. With bit 1 clear, a byte whose bit 0 is also clear, or equal to 17, takes entry `revision` of the float table at 0x420148 (1.125, 1.1); any other byte with bit 0 set takes entry `revision` of the table at 0x420150 (0.9375, 0.9166667). Neither table is bounds-checked, and they are adjacent, so a revision of 2 reads 0.9375 from the first.
  With bit 1 set, a non-zero byte at path `+301` skips the factor. Otherwise the signed halfwords at `+326` and `+328`, converted to float and divided, are compared as a double with 0.5 (0x420568) and a quotient other than 0.5 skips the factor; a quotient of exactly 0.5 sets the factor to 0.5 for a non-zero direction flag and to 2.0 (0x420580) for a zero one.
  When the factor was skipped the width is returned as it came (0x411f2c). Otherwise the width is halved as a signed divide (add the sign bit, shift right 1), converted to float, multiplied by the factor (`mul.s`), widened to double, added to 0.5 and truncated with `trunc.w.d`, and the result is doubled. When the word `mvpregtrace` is non-zero both paths then print "calcmitch( rev %d, xsize %d, dir %d, p %d, f %f ) = %d" (0x41a178) through `mvpPrintf` with the path, the revision, width, direction flag, `mvpPixSize` result, the factor as a double and the result. The routine returns the result.
- `mvpGetUSTPerMSC` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: asks the helper whether to halve a stored period, a 128-byte routine at address 0x413930. Established.
  Its second argument is the path and its fourth a pointer to a double. It calls `mvpUtilityRoutine` (0x41396c) with its first argument, the code 100, the path and its third argument. When that returns 2 it multiplies the double at the fourth argument by 0.5 (the constant at 0x420568, `mul.d`) and stores it back. It returns 0 on every path.
- `mvpGetFrontierMSC` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: fetches the driver's field-count value through the path, a 440-byte routine at address 0x4139b0. Established.
  Its second argument is the path and its fourth a pointer for the result. It loads the private pointer from path `+104` and calls `mvpAssertFail` with the text above, "mvpustmsc.c" (0x41a388) and line 46 when it is zero. A request record is built on the stack: the halfword 205 at `+0` and 232 at `+2` (the sizes the kernel's request table keys on), and the doubleword at `+16` set to the word at `+84` of the record that the path's word at `+20` points to when that word is non-zero, otherwise to the path's word at `+36`.
  `ioctl` (0x413a70) sends it with request 0x6d767002 on the descriptor at the private record's `+40`. A result other than -1 stores the doubleword at record `+16` through the fourth argument and returns 0. A result of -1 calls `oserror` (0x413a8c); a value of 7 calls `mvpRevisionError` with no argument set (0x413aa8). It then prints "mvpGetFrontierMSC %s: " plus newline (0x41a398) through `mvpErrPrintf` (0x413ae8) with the private pointer and the `strerror` text and returns 10 when the error number is 32 and 30 for any other number. The kernel handler for request 205 is `mvptransfer_get_frontier_msc` ([functions.md](functions.md)).
- `mvpGetUSTMSCPair` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: fetches a time and count pair through the path, a 404-byte routine at address 0x413b70. Established.
  The second argument is the path and the sixth a pointer to two doublewords. It asserts the private pointer as above with "mvpustmsc.c" and line 80, builds a record with the halfwords 206 at `+0` and 232 at `+2`, and sends it with `ioctl` (0x413c00) request 0x6d767002 on the descriptor at the private record's `+40`. A result other than -1 copies the doubleword at record `+8` to the first and the one at `+16` to the second doubleword of the pair and returns 0. A result of -1 follows the same ladder as `mvpGetFrontierMSC`, with the text "mvpGetUSTMSCPair %s: " plus newline (0x41a3b0), and returns 10 or 30 by the same rule. The kernel handler for request 206 is `mvptransfer_get_ustmsc_pair`.
- `mvpGetPathDelay` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: returns the constant 30, a 48-byte routine at address 0x413d10. Established.
  It stores its first seven argument registers into its stack frame, reads none of them back and returns 30 (`li v0,30` at 0x413d30).
- `myprint_leader` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: prints the lead-in of a register trace line, a 104-byte routine at address 0x411810. Established.
  When the word `mvpregtrace` (loaded through its GOT slot at 0x41181c) is zero it returns at once. Otherwise it picks the one-byte string "\n" (0x420340) when its argument is non-zero and the empty string (0x420342) when it is zero, and calls `printf` (0x411860) with "%sRegTrace: " (0x4199b0) and that string.
- `mvpreg_trace` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: prints one register access and then its decoded fields, a 176-byte routine at address 0x411c40. Established.
  The arguments are a flag, a text, a register name, an address and a 64-bit value. A non-zero flag first calls `myprint_leader` with 0 (the call at 0x411c84 is a `bgezall` on zero, which always links). When `mvpregtrace` is non-zero it then calls `printf` (0x411cbc) with the format "%s reg %s (0x%x) = 0x%llx (%llu)" plus newline (0x419a20), the text, the name, the address and the value twice. It always ends by calling `mvpreg_trace_fields` (0x411cd4) with the name and the value.
- `mvpreg_gbetrace` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: the same trace for a register that holds a 32-bit value, a 176-byte routine at address 0x411cf0. Established.
  Its steps and arguments are those of `mvpreg_trace`, except that the fifth argument is a 32-bit word: the format is "%s reg %s (0x%x) = 0x%lx (%ld)" plus newline (0x419a48), the word is printed twice from the stored word (`lw`), and the call to `mvpreg_trace_fields` (0x411d84) passes it zero-extended (`lwu`).
- `mvpreg_trace_fields` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: prints a register value broken into its named bit fields, a 948-byte routine at address 0x411880. Established.
  The arguments are a register name and a 64-bit value. It walks the table of name and descriptor pairs at 0x41f4e0 (34 pairs, ending with a null name pointer) comparing each name with the argument with `strcmp` (0x4118c0) and stops after the first match; with no match it prints nothing. On a match it adds 1 to the byte at 0x42016c and calls `myprint_leader` with 0.
  The descriptor is a word count followed by 12-byte records: a name pointer, a shift and a width. Each record's field is the value shifted right by the shift (`dsrlv`) and masked to the width, the mask being the complement of -2 shifted left by width minus 1. A name whose first byte is zero ends the loop early. When bit 5 of `mvpregtrace` is set the routine prints "rb: %2d, nb: %2d, val: 0x%016lx, rd: 0x%016lx, " with the shift, the width, the value and the shifted value, and "md: 0x%016lx, rd: 0x%016lx" with the mask and the field.
  The first byte of the name chooses the form. A byte 10 calls `myprint_leader` with a flag that is 1 once a plain field has been counted, skips the byte, and prints the field. A byte 7 is skipped when the field is zero and otherwise treated like a byte 10. A byte 9 is skipped when the field is zero and otherwise printed with the byte kept. A '#' skips the byte, and when the next byte is a digit 0 to 7 (`& 0xf8` equal to 0x30) it skips that too and shifts the field left by the digit; the field then prints as "%s:0x%x " and does not count. Every other name prints as "%s:%d " and adds 1 to the count. All printing needs a non-zero `mvpregtrace`. After the last record it subtracts 1 from the byte at 0x42016c, prints a newline when `mvpregtrace` is non-zero and returns.
  The table holds the library's own names for the bit fields of the video registers; [mace-video.md](mace-video.md#register-field-table-of-the-user-space-library) lists it.
- `mvpgeti2ccontrolindex` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: looks a control key up in a table and returns its index, a 160-byte routine at address 0x414000. Established.
  The argument is the key. The table at 0x41f8a0 has 101 entries of two words, a key and an index, and the routine compares the key with the first word of each entry from the first and returns the second word when they are equal; after the 101st it returns the 64-bit value -1. The keys run from 0x4d561001 to 0x4d561065 and the indexes from 0 to 652.
- `mvpgeti2cchip` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: returns the chip number that owns a control index, a 184-byte routine at address 0x4140a0. Established.
  The argument is a control index. It walks the global table `i2c_chip_index` (40 bytes, five entries of two words, at 0x41fbc8) from entry 0 and, for entry `i`, compares the argument as a signed value with the second word of entry `i+1`; when it is smaller it returns the first word of entry `i`. It ends with 0 when the first word of an entry is zero. The entries are (7111, 0), (7185, 217), (9001, 550), (9002, 642) and (0, 657), so indexes below 217 give 7111, below 550 give 7185, below 642 give 9001 and below 657 give 9002; what the numbers name is not shown by the code.
- `mvpSendI2CData` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: sends four 16-bit values to the I2C control request and reports the result, a 420-byte routine at address 0x415280. Established.
  The arguments are the path, a byte and a pointer to four halfwords. The record on the stack has the halfword 507 at `+0`, 144 at `+2`, the word 0x4d560004 at `+4`, 3 at `+8` and the fourth halfword at `+12`, the byte argument at `+36`, `mvpCDev` of it (0x4152e4) at `+37`, the four halfwords at `+40`, `+42`, `+44` and `+46`, and zero at `+48`. It prints "send i2c 0x%x 0x%x 0x%x 0x%x" plus newline (0x41a3c8) through `mvpPrintf` (call at 0x415358) with the path and the first, second, third and fourth halfword.
  `ioctl` (0x415378) then sends it with request 0x6d767005 on the descriptor at path `+40`. A result of -1 runs `oserror`; when that returns 7 it calls `mvpRevisionError` with no argument set (0x4153b0); it then calls `oserror` again, `strerror` and `mvpPrintf` with "mvpSendI2C: %s" plus newline (0x41a500), and returns 17. Any other result returns 0. The kernel handler for request 507 is `mvpcontrol_i2c_set`.
- `mvpSendI2C` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: the wrapper that unpacks a request structure for `mvpSendI2CData`, a 96-byte routine at address 0x415430. Established.
  The arguments are the path, a byte, a third that is stored and never read, and a structure pointer. It loads the word at structure `+12` and calls `mvpSendI2CData` (0x415474) with the path, the byte and that word as its third argument, and returns its result.
- `mvpGetI2CData` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: reads an I2C reply through the control request, a 368-byte routine at address 0x415490. Established.
  The arguments are the path, an unused second and a pointer to four halfwords. The record has the halfword 508 at `+0`, 144 at `+2`, the first three of the caller's halfwords at `+40`, `+42` and `+44` and zero at `+48`; `ioctl` (0x415504) sends it with request 0x6d767005 on the descriptor at path `+40`. A result of -1 runs the ladder of `mvpSendI2CData` with the text "mvpGetI2CData: %s" plus newline (0x41a510) and returns 17.
  On success it stores into the caller's fourth halfword (at `+6`) the third halfword (`+4`) ANDed with the halfword the driver left at record `+46`, prints "recv i2c 0x%x 0x%x 0x%x 0x%x" plus newline (0x41a448) through `mvpPrintf` (call at 0x4155d8) with the four halfwords and returns 0. The kernel handler for request 508 is `mvpcontrol_i2c_get`.
- `mvpControlIOCTL` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: sends one control request, a 228-byte routine at address 0x4078d0. Established.
  The arguments are the path, a record and a value. It stores 144 at record `+2`, the low byte of the value at `+36` and `mvpCDev` of the value at `+37` (call at 0x407904), then sends the record with `ioctl` (0x407930) request 0x6d767005 on the descriptor at path `+40`. A result of -1 calls `oserror`, calls `mvpRevisionError` with no argument set only when that returns 7, and then calls `mvpPrintf` with "mvpSetDeviceControl for default source event" plus newline (0x4189d8; the text names another routine) and returns 17; any other result returns 0.
- `mvpGetDeviceControls` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: fetches the driver's 72-byte control block into the path, a 216-byte routine at address 0x4080d0. Established.
  The argument is the path. When bit 9 (0x200) of path `+36` is clear it calls `mvpPanic` (0x40810c) with "mvpGetDeviceControls: not device path!" plus newline (0x418a38) and carries on. It clears a 144-byte record with `bzero`, stores 506 at its `+0` and calls `mvpControlIOCTL` (0x408144) with the path, the record and 0. A non-zero result is returned. Otherwise it copies 72 bytes from record `+24` to path `+288` with `bcopy` (source first) and returns 0. The kernel side of request 506 is in the request-code entry of `mvpcontrol_ioctl` above.
- `mvpGetCFI` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: asks for the video-format information block, a 72-byte routine at address 0x4079c0. Established.
  The arguments are the path and a record. It stores 511 at record `+0` and returns what `mvpControlIOCTL` returns for the path, the record and 0 (call at 0x4079f0, a `bgezall` on zero). The kernel side of request 511 is in the request-code entry of `mvpcontrol_ioctl` above; it returns the video information the CRIME driver supplies.
- `mvpGetScreenInfo` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: reads two numbers of the video-format block, a 172-byte routine at address 0x40b3f0. Established.
  The arguments are the path, an unused second, a selector and a pointer to two words. It calls `mvpGetCFI` (0x40b418) with the path and a record on the stack. For selector 4 it stores the halfword at record `+28` and the halfword at `+30` as the two words (sign-extended halfword loads) and returns the `mvpGetCFI` result. For selector 5 it stores the byte at record `+27` and the constant 1. Any other selector stores nothing. For a selector other than 4 the routine returns the selector itself, since `v0` was loaded from it before the comparison.
- `mvpGetDevPath` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: creates and sets up a path on the default device, a 232-byte routine at address 0x4106b0. Established.
  It takes no arguments and clears a 108-byte record on the stack. It calls `mvpCreatePath` (0x410708) with 0, the record, 0 and 0; a non-zero result returns 0. It then calls `mvpSetupPath` (0x41072c) with the record, 1 and 1; a non-zero result returns 0. It calls `mvpGetDeviceControls` (0x410744) with the word at record `+104`; a non-zero result calls `mvpPanic` with "mvpOpenVideo: mvpGetDeviceControls failed" (0x4192b0) and, if that returns, returns 0. Otherwise it returns the word at record `+104`.
- `mvpReleaseDevPath` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: tears down a path made by `mvpGetDevPath`, a 116-byte routine at address 0x4107a0. Established.
  The argument is the word to hand back. It clears a 108-byte record on the stack, stores the argument at record `+104` and calls `mvpDestroyPath` (0x4107fc) with 0 and the record, returning its result.
- `ddLoadDriver` in `usr/lib32/dmedia/video/mvp.so`: fills in the driver table and the node list for the default device, a 164-byte routine at address 0x410820. Established.
  The argument is the address of a word, into which it stores the address of the global `mvpdriver` (GOT load at 0x41083c, store at 0x410844). It calls `mvpGetDevPath` (0x410850); a zero result returns 6. Otherwise it calls `mvpUpdateNodes` (0x410878) with the address of the global `mvpdevices` and the path, then `mvpReleaseDevPath` (0x41088c) with the path, and returns 0.

# Venice and VS2 helpers of `setmon`, one entry each

Short routines of the 6.5.30 `usr/gfx/setmon` executable that belong to other graphics boards' code, not to CRIME or GBE; the one program carries every family's monitor routine. Addresses are its virtual addresses. Each was read in full from `objdump -d` with the strings taken from the file's data segments. A "direct call" means a `jal` to the routine's address.

- `generateFilename` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: joins two strings into a caller's buffer, a 68-byte routine at address 0x10012564. Established.
  Its third argument is the destination: the code moves it to the first argument of `sprintf`, moves the first two arguments to the third and fourth, and supplies the format "%s%s" from `.srdata` at 0x10042120. It returns what `sprintf` returns.
- `pipeCountOfSystem` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: asks the kernel how many graphics boards the system has, a 200-byte routine at address 0x100125a8. Established.
  It opens "/dev/graphics" with flags 0x802 (string at 0x1003a018). When the open fails it writes "ERROR: couldn't open /dev/graphics." to standard error (`__iob` plus 32) and returns 0. Otherwise it sends request 0x65 with a zero argument (`ioctl`, 0x100125ec) and, when that fails, writes "ERROR: couldn't get number of boards." and returns 0 without closing the descriptor. On success it closes the descriptor and returns the result of the request.
- `vs2Installed` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: tells whether the VS2 board passes its consistency check, a 56-byte routine at address 0x100129b4. Established.
  It calls `vs2ConsistencyCheck` (0x100126d8) with the argument it was given and returns 1 when that call returned 0 (`sltiu v0,v0,1`), otherwise 0.
- `vs2ReadByte` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: reads one byte of a VS2 board's register window, a 20-byte routine at address 0x10012bf0. Established.
  Its first argument is a record whose word at +4 holds the window's base address. The routine adds its second argument to that base and returns the unsigned byte loaded from the sum.
- `vs2WriteByte` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: writes one byte into the same register window, a 20-byte routine at address 0x10013108. Established.
  It adds its second argument to the base address in the word at +4 of its first argument and stores the low byte of its third argument there. It returns no value.
- `vs2WriteXilinxReg` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: writes one byte into the window at a two-part offset, a 24-byte routine at address 0x1001316c. Established.
  The address is the base in the word at +4 of the first argument plus the second argument plus the third; the stored byte is the low byte of the fourth argument. It returns no value.
- `vs2ToggleSerialClock` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: pulses the board's serial clock and reads back its state, an 88-byte routine at address 0x10013020. Established.
  It calls `vs2WriteByte` with its own first argument, offset 0x4280 and the value 1, then `vs2ReadByte` with the same record and offset 0x41a0, and returns the byte that read gave.
- `vs2WriteProgClock` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: writes a value to one of two programmable-clock offsets, an 80-byte routine at address 0x1001311c. Established.
  It passes its first and third arguments unchanged to `vs2WriteByte`. The offset is 0x41c0 when the second argument is 0x4200 and 0x41d0 for any other value.
- `getRMCount` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: reports how many raster-manager boards a Venice pipe has, a 56-byte routine at address 0x100133b4. Established.
  It reserves a 960-byte area on its stack, fills it by calling `getVeniceInfo` (0x10013184) with its argument and that area, and returns the word at +44 of the area.
- `get_ven_iptype` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: returns the Venice board type, probing it on first use, a 92-byte routine at address 0x1000fea0. Established.
  It returns the word `ven_iptype` when that is not -1. When it is -1 it calls `get_ven_type` (0x1000fdb0) and returns the word `ven_iptype` as `get_ven_type` left it.
- `venice_set_pipeaddr` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: records that the pipe is mapped and passes the pipe address on, a 60-byte routine at address 0x1000fefc. Established.
  It stores 1 in the global `ven_pipeMapped` in the delay slot of its call and then calls `fcg_venice_set_pipeaddr` (0x10010f84) without changing the arguments, returning that call's result.
- `venice_get_vcreg` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: reads a Venice register by index, a 52-byte routine at address 0x1000ff38. Established.
  It hands its argument to `fcg_venice_get_vcreg` (0x100110d4) and returns the value that routine read.
- `venice_set_vcreg` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: writes a Venice register by index, a 52-byte routine at address 0x1000ff6c. Established.
  It forwards both arguments to `fcg_venice_set_vcreg` (0x10011108) and returns what that routine returned.
- `fcg_venice_get_vcreg` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: loads one word of the graphics window, a 52-byte routine at address 0x100110d4. Established.
  The address is the word `ven_gfxvaddr` plus eight times the argument plus 0xfc000, and the word loaded there is the result.
- `fcg_venice_set_vcreg` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: stores one word into the graphics window, a 52-byte routine at address 0x10011108. Established.
  It uses the address of the read routine, `ven_gfxvaddr` plus eight times the first argument plus 0xfc000, and stores the second argument there. It returns no value.
- `fcg_get_gfx_fifo_count` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: computes the occupancy of the graphics command ring from two indices, a 64-byte routine at address 0x1001015c. Inferred.
  The base is the word `ven_pipeaddr` plus 0x10000. It loads the word at base minus 24536 and the word at base minus 24544, subtracts the second from the first and, when the first is the smaller as an unsigned number, adds 8192 to the difference before returning it. That the two words are a producer and a consumer index of an 8192-entry ring is inferred from the wrap.
- `_jt_select_chain` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the scan-chain selector entry point that leads to the Venice implementation, a 52-byte routine at address 0x10011acc. Established.
  It calls `fcg_jt_select_chain` (0x10011ff8) with the arguments it received and returns that call's value.
- `_jt_send_instruction` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the scan-chain instruction entry point, a 52-byte routine at address 0x10011b00. Established.
  Its only action is a direct call to `fcg_jt_send_instruction` (0x100120b4), whose result it returns.
- `_jt_send_data` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: the scan-chain data entry point, a 52-byte routine at address 0x10011b34. Established.
  Its only action is a direct call to `fcg_jt_send_data` (0x1001230c), whose result it returns.
- `vector_to_int` in `usr/gfx/setmon`: assembles four bytes into a word, a 48-byte routine at address 0x10011b68. Established.
  It reads the bytes at offsets 0 to 3 of its second argument and stores byte 0 as the lowest and byte 3 as the highest byte of the word that it writes through its first argument, so the result is the little-endian reading of the four bytes. It returns no value.

# Routines of `dualchannel`, one entry each

`dualchannel` (`usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`, o32, 6.5.30) talks to the dual-channel option board over the I2C driver of the CRIME kernel module. Its three bus helpers below each send the general I2C command (request 22027, 0x560b, in [ioctls.md](ioctls.md)) on the descriptor held in the global `i2cfd` (dynamic symbol 201), with a 16-byte record whose second byte is the command. The binary has no `.mdebug`, `.symtab` or DWARF; the names come from its dynamic symbol table. Each routine was read in full from `objdump -d` with the strings taken from `.rodata` and the imports named from the GOT formula (PLTGOT 0x10000b10 plus 4 times local_gotno 26 plus the dynamic symbol index minus the first global symbol 48).

- `crime_i2c_reset` in `usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`: asks the driver to reset the I2C bus and ends the program if that fails, a 188-byte routine at address 0x404780. Established.
  It takes no arguments. It zeroes byte 0 and stores 5 in byte 1 of a record on the stack (offsets 40 and 41 from the stack pointer), clears the word at record offset 12, and sends it with `ioctl` (dynamic symbol 60, call at 0x4047bc) on the descriptor loaded through the global `i2cfd`.
  A result that is not negative returns, leaving the `ioctl` result in `v0`. A negative result calls `perror` with the string "I2C_RESET failed" (0x100002d0), then calls `set_timing` (0x407888, dynamic symbol 170) with argument 0 only when the global `conf_test` (dynamic symbol 182) is non-zero, calls `close` on the saved descriptor and calls `exit` with 1.

- `crime_i2c_sendbyte` in `usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`: sends one byte on the I2C bus through the driver, a 172-byte routine at address 0x404c38. Established.
  The first argument's low byte is the byte to send. The routine builds a record on the stack with byte 0 zero, byte 1 equal to 2, the halfword at offset 2 equal to 1, the byte at offset 6 holding the argument and the word at offset 12 zero, and passes it to `ioctl` (call at 0x404c84) with request 22027 on the descriptor in `i2cfd`.
  A non-negative result returns 1. A negative result calls `fprintf` (dynamic symbol 64) on the stream at `__iob` plus 32 with the format "I2C_SENDBYTE failed, char = 0x%02x, " (0x10000340) and the byte, calls `perror` with the empty string at 0x10000368, and returns 0.

- `crime_i2c_rcvbytes` in `usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`: reads bytes from the I2C bus through the driver and reports how many came back, a 228-byte routine at address 0x404db4. Established.
  Its arguments are the number of bytes wanted, the buffer address and the address of an integer that receives the count. The record on the stack has byte 0 zero, byte 1 equal to 3, the halfword at offset 4 holding the wanted count and the word at offset 12 holding the buffer address; `ioctl` (call at 0x404dfc) sends it with request 22027 on the descriptor in `i2cfd`.
  A negative result prints "I2C_RCVBYTES failed, rval = %d" (0x10000398) with that result to the stream at `__iob` plus 32 (`fprintf`, call at 0x404e20) and goes on. The routine then reads the halfword at offset 4 back as the number received, stores it through the third argument, and when it is smaller than the number wanted prints "I2C_RCVBYTES failed, reply len = %d, " (0x100003b8) with it (`fprintf`, call at 0x404e60) and calls `perror` with the empty string at 0x100003e0. It returns the number received on every path; the comparison is a branch-likely `beql`, whose delay slot loads the saved return address.

- `crime_i2c_start` in `usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`: asks the driver to put a start condition on the I2C bus and ends the program if that fails, a 184-byte routine at address 0x40490c. Established.
  It takes no arguments. It builds the same 16-byte record as `crime_i2c_reset` on the stack with byte 0 and byte 1 both zero (stores at 0x404930 and 0x404934, record offsets 40 and 41 from the stack pointer) and the word at record offset 12 zero, and sends it with `ioctl` (call at 0x404944) request 22027 on the descriptor loaded through `i2cfd`.
  A result that is not negative returns, leaving the `ioctl` result in `v0`. A negative result calls `perror` with the string "I2C_START failed" (0x100002f8; call at 0x40495c), calls `set_timing` (call at 0x404980) with argument 0 only when the global `conf_test` is non-zero, calls `close` on the saved descriptor (0x404994) and calls `exit` with 1 (0x4049a8). The only difference from `crime_i2c_stop` is byte 1, which is 1 there.

- `crime_i2c_stop` in `usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`: asks the driver to put a stop condition on the I2C bus and ends the program if that fails, a 188-byte routine at address 0x404a98. Established.
  It takes no arguments. The record on the stack has byte 0 zero, byte 1 equal to 1 (the store at 0x404ac4; `t6` is loaded with 1 at 0x404ab8) and the word at record offset 12 zero; `ioctl` (call at 0x404ad4) sends it with request 22027 on the descriptor in `i2cfd`.
  A result that is not negative returns, leaving the `ioctl` result in `v0`. A negative result runs the same failure steps as `crime_i2c_start` in the same order, with the string "I2C_STOP failed" (0x10000320) for `perror` (call at 0x404aec), `set_timing` at 0x404b10 (only when `conf_test` is non-zero), `close` at 0x404b24 and `exit` with 1 at 0x404b38.

- `crime_i2c_test` in `usr/sbin/Confidence/dualchannel`: writes three bytes to the I2C bus again and again and never returns, a 332-byte routine at address 0x404fac. Established.
  It takes no arguments. It prints "Continuous I2c write test:" and a newline (0x10000430) with `fprintf` (call at 0x404fe8) on the stream at `__iob` plus 32, then loads the descriptor from `i2cfd` into `s1` and keeps a pointer to a 16-byte record on the stack (offset 48). It zeroes byte 0 and the word at record offset 12, stores 5 in byte 1 and sends it with `ioctl` request 22027 (call at 0x405014), the bus-reset command of `crime_i2c_reset`.
  The loop starts at 0x405028. It stores 0x7e at record offset 6, 2 in byte 1 and 1 in the halfword at offset 2 (the send-one-byte record of `crime_i2c_sendbyte`) and calls `ioctl` (0x405048), then replaces the byte at offset 6 with 0xaa (`ioctl` at 0x405068) and with 0x55 (`ioctl` at 0x405088). It then stores 1 in byte 1 (the stop command of `crime_i2c_stop`) and calls `ioctl` a fifth time (0x4050a8). No `ioctl` result is read.
  A counting loop follows: `v0` starts at zero and the branch-likely `bnel` at 0x4050bc, whose delay slot adds 4, repeats until `v0` equals 0xc350 (50000, so 12500 additions, as the first addition is the instruction before the branch). The routine then branches back to 0x405028 with 0x7e loaded in the delay slot. The return sequence at 0x4050e0 to 0x4050f4 is unreachable. The routine never stores record bytes 4, 5 and 7 to 11, so they hold whatever the stack had.

# Routines of `symmon.IP32`: firmware-vector slots, empty stubs and the debugger's own helpers

These entries cover the small routines of `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32` (6.5.30, N32). Most are eight bytes: `jr ra` and one delay-slot instruction. Where a table or a `jal` reaches them, the entry names the sites. Where none does, it says so, after a search of the whole image for `jal`, `j`, an address formed with `lui` and `addiu` or `ori`, and a word of `.data`, `.rodata` or `.sdata` holding the address. The firmware vector referred to below is the 140-byte table at 0xa003bf28 that `_init_spb` (0xa00271fc) copies to 0xa0001800, and its 72-byte continuation at 0xa003bfb8 that goes to 0xa0001c00. `_init_spb` does that only when the word at 0xa005a3e0 is non-zero, and it records both destinations at 0xa0001020 and 0xa0001028.

- `Invoke` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the second slot of the firmware vector, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf54. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and does nothing else (`jr ra` with `move v0,zero` in the delay slot). Its only reference is the word at 0xa003bf2c, between `Load` (0xa001bf4c) and `Execute` (0xa001bf5c), which lands at 0xa0001804 after the copy. No `jal` or `j` targets it, so the debugger never starts a program through this slot.
- `Execute` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the third slot of the firmware vector, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf5c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  Its delay-slot `move v0,zero` makes it return 0 without reading an argument or touching memory. The word at 0xa003bf30 holds its address, before `Halt` (0xa002bd28), and the copy puts it at 0xa0001808. Nothing in the image calls it directly.
- `exec_abs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the vector slot for running an absolute-address image, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf74. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and executes nothing. The word at 0xa003bfc8 points at it, after `invoke_abs` (0xa001bf6c) and before `fs_register` (0xa0026388); that word is the fifth entry of the second table and lands at 0xa0001c10. No code calls it.
- `GetPeer` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: returns the next sibling of a configuration-tree node, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00241c0. Established.
  It loads the word at offset 40 of its first argument in the delay slot of the return and does not test the argument for null, whereas the neighbouring `GetChild` (0xa00241c8) does. The vector word at 0xa003bf4c holds it (copied to 0xa0001824). Nine `jal` sites call it: 0xa00245d8 and 0xa00245fc (the unlabelled routine at 0xa0024594, after `SaveConfiguration`), 0xa0026810 (the unlabelled routine at 0xa0026790, after `_init_saio`), 0xa002b304 (`getcpuid`), 0xa0030764 and 0xa00307b0 (the recursive walker at 0xa0030700), 0xa0030834 (`find_type`), 0xa00308b8 (`count_type`) and 0xa0031674 (unlabelled code after `atobu_L`). In the walker at 0xa0030700, a step into `GetChild` and a recursive step come before the call to this routine and a second recursive step.
- `GetParent` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: returns the parent of a configuration-tree node, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00241e4. Established.
  The delay-slot `lw v0,36(a0)` reads the word at offset 36 of its argument, with no null check. The vector word at 0xa003bf54 sits between `GetChild` (0xa00241c8) and `GetConfigurationData` (0xa00241ec), and lands at 0xa000182c. Three sites call it: 0xa0030404 in `fixupname` and 0xa00305d4 in `inv_findtape`, which each read the word at offset 4 of the returned node and compare it (with 16 in `fixupname`), and 0xa0030090 in the unlabelled routine at 0xa0030074 that passes the result on as a first argument.
- `cpuid` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reports the number of the processor running the code, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c5c. Established.
  It always returns 0, so this image has one logical processor. It is the 47th word of the firmware vector (0xa003bfe0), which lands at 0xa0001c28 in the continuation table. Seventy-five `jal` sites call it with a nop in the delay slot, among them `symmon_exchandler` (first at 0xa0019f44, whose result is shifted left 9 to index a 512-byte-per-processor record at 0xa0058db8), `_PrintInstruction` (ten sites), `printf` (five sites, the first at 0xa002e660), `putchar`, `puts`, `sprintf` and the `vprintf` and `ttyprintf` families. `printf` calls it only when the word at 0xa005a3e4 is non-zero (otherwise it uses 0), shifts the result left 7 and indexes the 128-byte records at the pointer stored at 0xa003c910.
- `businfo` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the vector slot for describing the system bus, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00284ec. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  The body is `jr ra` and a `nop`, so it leaves `v0` as the caller left it and writes nothing. It is the 48th word of the vector (0xa003bfe4), following `cpuid`, and lands at 0xa0001c2c; `cpufreq` (0xa00281dc) follows it in the table. `read_reg64` (0xa00284f4) is the next routine in the text. No `jal` targets it.
- `_errgetc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: supplies the end-of-input value for a one-character read, an 8-byte routine at 0xa002a890. Established.
  It returns -1 (`addiu v0,zero,-1` in the delay slot). `getchar` (0xa002d310) and `getc` (0xa002d368) each call `Read` (0xa00251ec) for one byte and take this call at 0xa002d344 and 0xa002d398 when `Read` returns non-zero or the count stored at sp+4 is not 1; otherwise they return the byte at sp+0 loaded with `lbu`.
- `_init_mbufs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the network buffer set-up hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bec4. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It is an immediate return with a `nop` delay slot, so no buffer pool is built. `_init_saio` (0xa002664c) calls it at 0xa0026744, right after `fs_init` (0xa002633c) and before `_init_sockets`. It is the only caller.
- `_init_sockets` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the socket-table set-up hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001becc. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  Its whole body is `jr ra` over a `nop`. It has one caller, the `jal` at 0xa002674c in `_init_saio` (0xa002664c), between `_init_mbufs` and `_init_bootenv` (0xa0026fa8), so the debugger starts with no sockets.
- `_init_mouse` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the pointing-device set-up hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df28. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and reads no hardware. `_init_saio` calls it at 0xa0026764 and discards the result; the call at 0xa002676c that follows goes to `_init_systemid` (0xa00257e0). `vh_checksum` (0xa001df30) follows it in the text.
- `_m_get` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the network-buffer allocator slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf1c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It hands back 0, a null buffer, for any request. It sits between `logoOff` (0xa001bf14) and `_m_freem` (0xa001bf24) in a run of stubs, and nothing in the image calls it or stores its address.
- `_m_freem` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the network-buffer release slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf24. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  The body is `jr ra` and a `nop`: it frees nothing and returns no value. It is unreferenced, with `_m_get` before it and `ether_sprintf` (0xa001bf2c) after.
- `ether_sprintf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the Ethernet-address formatter slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf2c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It writes no text and returns 0, so a caller would receive a null string pointer. It has no caller and no table entry. Its neighbours are `_m_freem` (0xa001bf24) and `_so_append` (0xa001bf34).
- `_so_append` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the socket-queue append slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf34. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 with `move v0,zero` in the delay slot and has no side effects. No `jal`, `j` or table word reaches it. `ether_sprintf` precedes it and `_find_socket` (0xa001bf3c) follows.
- `_find_socket` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the socket lookup slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf3c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  Every lookup would return 0, meaning no socket found. The routine is never called, and the next routine in the text is `load_init` (0xa001bf44), then `Load` (0xa001bf4c), the first vector slot.
- `_null_strat` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the strategy routine of the null device, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf7c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 without looking at the request. Between `exec_abs` (0xa001bf74) and `nullsetup` (0xa001bf84), it is called by nothing and no table holds its address.
- `_mem_strat` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the strategy routine of the memory device, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf8c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0, so a transfer request to it moves no data and reports no error code. `nullsetup` precedes it and `memsetup` (0xa001bf94) follows. It has no caller and no table word.
- `_tpscopen` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the open routine of the SCSI tape driver slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001def0. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0, so an open of that device reports success without touching a controller. `_tpscclose` (0xa001def8) is the next routine. Nothing calls it or stores its address.
- `_tpscclose` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the close routine of the SCSI tape driver slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001def8. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and does nothing else. It lies between `_tpscopen` and `dksc_strat` (0xa001df00) and has no references.
- `dksc_strat` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the strategy routine of the SCSI disk driver slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df00. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  The delay-slot `move v0,zero` makes it return 0 for every request, so it queues nothing. It is followed by `ms_config` (0xa001df08). No caller and no table word refers to it.
- `_mspoll` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the mouse polling hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df20. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and polls no device. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa001ec5c, in the unlabelled code that follows `txConfig` (0xa001e4bc, 0x178 bytes), which makes the call with the argument it loaded from sp+40 and does not use the result.
- `bell` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the audible-alert hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c30. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and sounds nothing. The only call is the `jal` at 0xa001fcbc in the unlabelled code after `txConfig` (0xa001e4bc), made with no argument and followed by a branch to the common exit at 0xa001fc20. `cpu_savecfg` (0xa0020c38) is the next routine.
- `cpu_show_fault` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the processor-fault display hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c54. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 without printing anything. It sits between `cpu_savecfg` and `cpuid` (0xa0020c5c), and nothing calls it.
- `eerom_read` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the serial-EEPROM read hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c64. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It reads no EEPROM and returns 0, which a caller would take as a zero value. It has no caller. `cpu_nv_lock` (0xa0020c6c) follows it.
- `cpu_nv_lock` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the non-volatile-memory lock hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c6c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  The body returns 0 and takes no lock. It is unreferenced, between `eerom_read` and `cpu_set_nvram` (0xa0020c74).
- `cpu_set_nvram` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the non-volatile-memory write hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c74. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and writes no byte. Neither a call nor a table word reaches it, and `ecc_error_decode` (0xa0020c7c) follows.
- `ecc_error_decode` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the memory-error decode hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020c7c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 and decodes nothing. It is the last of the eight-byte routines in this run and the next routine in the text is the 200-byte `arcs_format_module_id`. No one calls it.
- `cpu_probe` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the processor-detection hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0011404. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0 with the `move` in its delay slot. `mpsetup` (0xa001be20) calls it first, at 0xa001be28, and ignores the result. The same routine then goes on to `init_symmon_gpda` (0xa001b394), `_hook_exceptions` (0xa0029a60) and `_save_exceptions` (0xa00297d4). It sits immediately before `_ip32_disable_serial_dma` (0xa001140c), and nothing else references it.
- `cpu_reset` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the processor-reset hook of start-up, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0027460. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns at once over a `nop` and resets nothing. `_init_saio` (0xa002664c) calls it at 0xa0026688, after `_init_spb` (0xa00271fc) and before its `initlock` call (0xa002a74c) on the record at 0xa003c770.
- `bp_try_stop_cpus` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the multiprocessor stop request for a breakpoint, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001a714. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It reports 0, which both callers hand straight back: `_kp` (0xa001a474) at 0xa001a570 moves it into `v0` through the branch to 0xa001a514, and `_kpcheck` (0xa001a5b8) does the same through 0xa001a674 from the `jal` at 0xa001a698. No other processor is signalled. The unlabelled routine at 0xa001a71c follows it.
- `dump_gpda` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the per-processor data dump hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0029004. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns immediately and prints nothing. The next routine, at 0xa002900c, opens a 16-byte stack frame and reaches `_ticksper1024inst` (0xa0029174). Nothing refers to `dump_gpda`.
- `checksum_rb` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the restart-block checksum routine, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0031310. Established.
  It returns 0 whatever block it is given. Both callers store that result in the word at offset 32 of the 552-byte block: `init_rb` (0xa00311b8) at 0xa0031244 after it fills offset 0 with 0x42545352 and offsets 4, 8 and 10 with 552, 1 and 10, and `rbsetbs` (0xa0031294) in the delay slot at 0xa00312d8. The stored checksum is therefore always 0.
- `cvsema` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the condition-variable semaphore slot, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00311b0. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns 0, unlike the `suser` stub (0xa00311a8) before it, which returns 1. It is a separate address from the 22-symbol stub at 0xa00311a0 and has no callers.
- `exceptutlb` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the user-TLB-miss entry published to the firmware, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bed4. Established.
  It returns at once, over a `nop`. `_init_spb` forms its address with `lui` and `addiu` (-16684) at 0xa0027264 and stores it at 0xa0001018, and `_hook_exceptions` (0xa0029a60) stores it at 0x80001018 in the delay slot of its second `__cache_wb_inval` call at 0xa0029b8c. It follows `_init_sockets` and precedes `exceptnorm`.
- `exceptnorm` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the general-exception entry published to the firmware, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bedc. Established.
  Its body is `jr ra` with a `nop`. `_init_spb` stores its address (0xa0020000 minus 16676) at 0xa0001014 with the `sw` at 0xa0027288, and `_hook_exceptions` stores it at 0x80001014 with the `sw` at 0xa0029b84. The debugger's real exception entry is `exception` (0xa001041c), so this slot only fills the table.
- `_setenv` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the common back end of the environment-variable setters, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bef4. Established.
  It stores nothing and returns 0. Three one-line wrappers call it, each passing its own arguments through and setting the third in the delay slot: `SetEnvironmentVariable` (0xa00259dc) and `setenv` (0xa002ba2c) with 0, and `syssetenv` (0xa002ba48) with 1. All three return its 0, so the firmware interface reports success while no variable changes.
- `db_printf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the debugger's print entry that forwards to the C library, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0010348. Established.
  The `j` at 0xa0010348 tail-jumps to `printf` (0xa002e620) with a `nop` delay slot, leaving every argument register as it arrived. Nothing calls it or holds its address.
- `_quit` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the debugger's quit command, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0010340. Established.
  Its single `jal` at 0xa0010340 calls `EnterInteractiveMode` (0xa0031758), which calls through the word at offset 28 of the vector that 0xa0001020 points to. There is no `jr ra` after the call, so a return would run on into `db_printf`. It is the handler word of the command-table record at 0xa00390a4 (name "quit" at 0xa0035838, help text at 0xa0035840). `start` reaches it with the `j` at 0xa0010338, to the next word, after `_dbgmon` (0xa001bb68) returns, and `_rdebug` calls it at 0xa001d468 after closing its descriptor.
- `freesplock` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the spin-lock release hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa002a75c. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It returns immediately and releases nothing, and no `jal`, `j` or data word names it. `initnlock` (0xa002a754) precedes it and `splockspl` (0xa002a764) follows.

# Routines of `symmon.IP32`: empty library replacements and vector slots, second group

These entries cover more of the small routines of `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32` (6.5.30, N32). Most are `jr ra` with one delay-slot instruction. Each says which table word, `jal` or other reference reaches it, from a search of the whole image for `jal`, `j`, an address built with `lui` and `addiu` or `ori`, and a word of `.data`, `.rodata` or `.sdata` holding the address. The private vector is the 72-byte table at 0xa003bfb8 that `_init_spb` (0xa00271fc) copies to 0xa0001c00; slot n lands at 0xa0001c00 plus 4n.

- `run_cached` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the hook that moves execution to the cached address window, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00114b0. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the symbol alone.
  It is `jr ra` and a `nop`, so no switch to cached execution happens. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa0027340 in `cpu_get_eaddr` (0xa0027314), which the `beqz` at 0xa0027324 reaches when the word at 0xa005a3e0 is zero; the routine then branches back to 0xa002732c and goes on to `ds2502_get_eaddr` (0xa0028de4).
- `setenv_nvram` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the non-volatile RAM variable writer, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bee4 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It returns 0 (`move v0,zero` in the delay slot of `jr ra`) and reads neither of its two string arguments, so no variable is stored and the caller is told it succeeded. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word in the image reaches it.
- `get_nvram_tab` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the vector slot for copying out the non-volatile RAM variable table, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001beec. Established.
  It returns 0 (`move v0,zero` in the delay slot) and reads neither argument, so nothing is copied. The only reference is the word at 0xa003bfbc, the second slot of the private vector that `_init_spb` (0xa00271fc) copies to 0xa0001c00 (so it lands at 0xa0001c04); `ioctl` (0xa0026ba0) is the slot before it and `load_abs` (0xa001bf64) the slot after.
- `scsiinit` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the machine-dependent SCSI set-up hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001befc. Established.
  The body is `jr ra` over a `nop`, with no arguments and no result. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word in the image reaches it. The debugger's board has no such call.
- `guiCheckMouse` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the graphics-console mouse callback, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf04. Established.
  It is `jr ra` and a `nop`: it ignores its three arguments and returns. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word in the image reaches it, so nothing installs it as a callback.
- `initGfxGui` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the graphics-console start-up hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf0c. Established.
  It is `jr ra` and a `nop`: it sets no register and ignores its argument. Its one caller is `_init_htp` (0xa002aa10), whose `jal` at 0xa002aa3c passes the word at `htp` (0xa003aa98, the word `gethtp` returns) in its delay slot. `_init_htp` reaches that call when the word at 0xa0058d1c is non-zero on entry. Otherwise it calls the routine at 0xa002a898 with 0, stores 1 in 0xa0058d1c unless that routine returned -1 (then 0), has `htp_init` (0xa001fda0) run when the result was not -1, and reloads the word and enters this routine if it is now non-zero. It returns the word at 0xa0058d1c on both paths.
- `logoOff` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the hook that turns the start-up logo off, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf14. Established.
  The body is `jr ra` and a `nop` with no argument or result. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word reaches it.
- `load_init` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the program-loader start-up hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf44. Established.
  It is `jr ra` and a `nop` and takes no arguments. The only caller is the `jal` at 0xa0026714 in `_init_saio` (0xa002664c), after `md_init` (0xa00292c8) and before `_init_alarm` (0xa0024d80).
- `load_abs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the vector slot for loading an image to an absolute address, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf64. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) and leaves its output untouched. The word at 0xa003bfc0 is the third slot of the private vector (0xa0001c08 after the copy), between `get_nvram_tab` (0xa001beec) and `invoke_abs` (0xa001bf6c).
- `invoke_abs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the vector slot for starting an image at an absolute address, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf6c. Established.
  Its delay-slot `move v0,zero` makes it return 0 without reading any of its five arguments, so nothing is started. The word at 0xa003bfc4 holds its address (the fourth slot of the private vector, landing at 0xa0001c0c), after `load_abs` (0xa001bf64) and before `exec_abs` (0xa001bf74).
- `nullsetup` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the set-up routine of the `null` pseudo-device, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf84. Established.
  Its delay-slot `move v0,zero` returns 0 with the three arguments unread. Its address is the third word of the record at 0xa003bec4 in `config_desc` (name pointer 0xa0036ec0, the string `null`, the number 4, then this routine), the record after `memsetup`'s at 0xa003beb8 and before the zero word at 0xa003bed0 that ends the table walk of `checkpseudo` (0xa0025d98).
- `memsetup` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the set-up routine of the `mem` pseudo-device, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001bf94. Established.
  It returns 0 (`move v0,zero`) and ignores its three arguments, so no node is built. Its address is the third word of the 12-byte record at 0xa003beb8 in `config_desc`: the name pointer 0xa0036eb8 (the string `mem`), the number 3, then this routine. The record is read by the unlabelled routine at 0xa0025d98 (`checkpseudo` in the debug information) on a path that has found a '(' in the device string: it takes the name length as the distance to the '(', walks the 12-byte records until a zero name word, skips records whose length word differs, and for a record whose name matches by `strncmp` calls the third word with its third argument, its second argument and a pointer to the '(' text, returning the result.
- `tpsc_strat` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the tape driver's strategy entry, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001ded8. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) for any request and reads neither argument. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word reaches it, so no node is registered with it.
- `tpsctapetype` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the tape-model lookup, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001dee0. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) and does not read the inquiry-data buffer it is given, so every drive would be type 0. It is the second of the five tape stubs listed under `tpsc_strat` (0xa001ded8). Nothing in the image calls it.
- `tpsc_tapeid` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the tape identification routine, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001dee8. Established.
  The body returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) and uses no argument.
- `ms_config` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the mouse driver's configuration hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df08. Established.
  It returns 20 (`addiu v0,zero,20` in the delay slot) and never reads its argument, so it adds no mouse node. The value is the no-such-device error of the firmware's error list. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word reaches it.
- `ms_install` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the mouse driver's reset hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df10. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) and has no arguments or side effects. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word reaches it.
- `ms_input` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the mouse driver's input-byte hook, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df18. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) without reading the byte it is given. Nothing in the image calls it or stores its address.
- `vh_checksum` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the volume-header checksum, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df30. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`) and reads no header, so no sum of the volume header is computed. Nothing in the image calls it.
- `is_vh` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the volume-header check, an 8-byte routine at 0xa001df38. Established.
  It returns 0 (delay-slot `move v0,zero`), so every header is rejected, and it reads no argument. The unlabelled routine `txhexcolor` of the debug information starts at 0xa001df40. Nothing in the image calls it.
- `txSetJumpbuf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: stores the jump buffer for the graphics console's button handler, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020030. Established.
  It is `jr ra` over a `nop` and stores nothing. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002ad10 in the graphics ioctl routine at 0xa002ac94 (`_gfxioctl`), taken when the request word at offset 12 of the I/O block equals 0x6701; the delay slot passes the word at offset 16 of the block. `txSetString` (0xa0020038) follows it and `gethtp` (0xa0020040) comes after that.
- `txSetString` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: stores the button-press string for the graphics console, an 8-byte routine at 0xa0020038. Established.
  The body is `jr ra` and a `nop`; the argument is ignored. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002acf4 in the graphics ioctl routine at 0xa002ac94 (`_gfxioctl`), taken when the request word at offset 12 of the I/O block equals 0x6702, with the word at offset 16 of the block as its argument.
- `sgivers` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the vector slot that reports the private-interface version, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00271a4. Established.
  It returns 3 (`addiu v0,zero,3` in the delay slot). The word at 0xa003bfdc holds its address as the tenth slot of the private vector (0xa0001c24 after the copy), between `gethtp` (0xa0020040) and `cpuid` (0xa0020c5c). The code after it, from 0xa00271ac, is the unlabelled routine `_setup_spb` of the debug information.
- `_delayend` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: a one-word marker, a 4-byte routine at 0xa0029140. Established.
  The word is a `nop` (all zeros) and there is no return, so execution would run into `_timerticksper1024inst` (0xa0029144) after it; zero words precede it from the code that ends at 0xa0029108. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word in the image reaches it. Here `_ticksper1024inst` (0xa0029174) lies after `_delayend` and is reached by a plain `jal` at 0xa0029014, and nothing takes the address of `_delayend`, so no such copy exists in this image.
- `initlock` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the spin-lock initialiser, an 8-byte routine at 0xa002a74c. Established.
  It is `jr ra` over a `nop` and touches neither the lock it is given nor any register. Two `jal` sites call it: 0xa00114c4 in `init_brkpt_lock` (0xa00114b8), whose delay slot builds 0xa003cc28 (`lui` 0xa004 and `addiu` -13272) as the argument, and 0xa0026694 in `_init_saio` (0xa002664c), whose delay slot builds 0xa003c770 (`addiu` -14480), and `init_cfgtree` (0xa0024084) runs next.
- `initnlock` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the named spin-lock initialiser, an 8-byte routine at 0xa002a754. Established.
  The body is `jr ra` and a `nop`; it does not call the empty `initlock` (0xa002a74c) beside it. No `jal`, `j`, address pair or data word reaches it.
- `suser` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the privilege test, an 8-byte routine at 0xa00311a8. Established.
  It returns 1 (`addiu v0,zero,1` in the delay slot), so every caller is treated as privileged. It follows the 22-name stub at 0xa00311a0 and precedes `cvsema` (0xa00311b0), which returns 0.
- `_gfxinit` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: resets the graphics console's open count on a soft reset, a 16-byte static routine at 0xa002aabc (the range of `_gfxinit` in the debug information). Established.
  It stores zero in the word at 0xa0059720 (the open count, `lui` 0xa006 and offset -26848, stored in the delay slot of the return) and returns 0 (`move v0,zero` before the `lui`).
- `_gfxopen` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: opens the graphics console, an 84-byte static routine at 0xa002aacc. Established.
  The argument is the I/O record. It loads the flag word at offset 44 and, when bit 0x1000 is set (the extended-console flag, which `_gfxReopen` also uses), stores 7 at offset 32 of the record and returns 7. Otherwise it saves the record in `s0`, calls `_init_htp` (0xa002aa10) with no arguments, then calls `_gfxReopen` (0xa002ab20) with the record and a second argument of 0, and returns that call's result. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002ad98 in `_gfx_strat`, taken for function code 1. The shipped body has the colour-console test compiled in.
- `_gfxclose` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: closes the graphics console, a 36-byte static routine at 0xa002ac18. Established.
  It loads the open count at 0xa0059720 and, only when it equals 1, stores zero there (the store is in the delay slot of the `b` at 0xa002ac34). It returns 0 on both paths and never reads its argument, so a close with the count above 1 leaves the count as it was. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002adb8 in `_gfx_strat` (0xa002ad30), taken for function code 2.
- `_gfxReopen` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: (re)initialises the graphics console and counts opens, a 248-byte static routine at 0xa002ab20. Established.
  The first argument is the I/O record and the second says whether to run the hardware set-up. If the word at offset 4 of the record (the unit) is non-zero it stores 20 at offset 32 and returns 20. With a zero second argument it loads the open count at 0xa0059720: a non-zero count is incremented and the routine returns 0 without touching the hardware. Otherwise (second argument non-zero, or count zero) it stores zero at 0xa0059724 (the flag `_gfxwrite` and `_gfxioctl` test), calls the routine at 0xa002a898 with its second argument (`htp_gfx_init` in the debug information) and compares the result with -1. On -1 it decrements the open count, sets the environment variable `gfx` (string at 0xa00376c0) to `dead` (0xa00376c8) with `syssetenv` (0xa002ba40), stores 8 at offset 32 of the record and returns 8. Otherwise it calls `getenv` (0xa002ba5c) for `gfx`, sets it to `alive` (0xa00376d0) first when it was unset, sets it to `alive` again with the store of 1 to 0xa0059724 in that call's delay slot, increments the open count and returns 0. The callers are the `jal` at 0xa002ab0c in `_gfxopen` (second argument 0) and the `jal` at 0xa002ad20 in `_gfxioctl` (second argument 1).
- `_gfxwrite` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes a buffer to the graphics console, an 88-byte static routine at 0xa002ac3c. Established.
  The argument is the I/O record. When the flag word at 0xa0059724 is zero it stores 20 at offset 32 of the record and returns 20. Otherwise it calls `ioctl` (0xa0026ba0) with descriptor 0, request 0x740d and a zero argument (the check-for-stop request), then `txPrint` (0xa002aa90) with the word at offset 12 as the data pointer and the word at offset 16 as the count, ignores `txPrint`'s result, and returns 0. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002ada8 in `_gfx_strat` (0xa002ad30), for function code 4.
- `_gfxioctl` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: handles control requests for the graphics console, a 156-byte static routine at 0xa002ac94. Established.
  The argument is the I/O record; the request code is the word at offset 12, its argument the word at offset 16 and the error slot offset 32. When the flag word at 0xa0059724 is zero it stores 20 in the error slot and returns 20. Otherwise request 0x7404 (the reopen request) with a zero argument calls `_gfxReopen` (0xa002ab20) with the record and 1 and returns its result, and with a non-zero argument returns 0 with no call. Request 0x6701 calls `txSetJumpbuf` (0xa0020030) and 0x6702 `txSetString` (0xa0020038), each with the argument, and both return 0. Any other request stores 7 in the error slot and returns 7. The one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002ad78 in `_gfx_strat`, for function code 16.
- `__dprintf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints a debugging message only when verbose mode is on, a 40-byte static routine at 0xa001d148. Established.
