  It loads the word at 0xa0059744 (the second of three words `_init_saio` sets with `atob` from environment variables, here the one read from the string `VERBOSE` at 0xa0036f98) and, when it is non-zero, calls `printf` (0xa002e620) with the arguments it received (it changes none of the argument registers), then returns; when it is zero it returns at once without calling. The result of `printf` is not copied anywhere, so `v0` holds whatever `printf` left, or the caller's own value on the quiet path. Six `jal` sites call it: 0xa001cafc, 0xa001cc70, 0xa001cc84 and 0xa001cc98 (inside `getpkt`, 0xa001ca30), 0xa001cd80 (inside `csum_getc` of the debug information, 0xa001ccc8) and 0xa001ce74 (inside `putpkt`, 0xa001cdf0), all in the serial protocol code.
- `_count_CPUs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: counts processor nodes in the firmware configuration tree below a node, a 116-byte static routine at 0xa003163c. Established.
  The argument is a tree node. The first count is 1 when the word at offset 0 of the node is 1 and the word at offset 4 is 1, and 0 otherwise. It then takes `GetChild` (0xa00241c8) of the node and, for each child in turn, adds the result of a recursive call and moves on with `GetPeer` (0xa00241c0) until that returns zero. It returns the sum. The callers are the recursive `jal` at 0xa0031668 and the `jal` at 0xa00316e0 in `_get_numcpus` (0xa00316b0), which passes `GetChild` of the null node.
- `_delete_str` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: removes the first `name=value` string whose name matches, from a string table, a 260-byte static routine at 0xa002b424. Established.
  The arguments are the name (a0, held in `s3`), the table (a1, `s2`) and an optional name buffer (a2, `s4`). It takes `strlen` (0xa00309ac) of the name, then scans the word-sized pointers from the start of the table up to the count held in the word at offset 1540 of the table, comparing each string's first name-length bytes with `strncasecmp` (0xa0030224) and requiring a `=` (61) right after. At the first match, when a buffer was given and the name length is below 79, it copies the name length bytes with `strncpy` (0xa0030db0) and ends them with a zero byte. It then moves each later pointer one place down, including the zero pointer that ends the list, and decrements the count at offset 1540. No match means no change. It returns no value. The routine at 0xa002b408 (`delete_str`) calls it with a zero buffer (the `jal` at 0xa002b410) and `replace_str` (0xa002b3b0) calls it at 0xa002b3cc.
- `_clear_cache` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: flushes the data cache for a range of virtual addresses, including mapped kernel addresses, a 292-byte static routine at 0xa001b270. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0, kept in `s0`) and the length (a1, `s1`). An address from 0xffffffff80000000 up to but not including 0xffffffffa0000000 (the cached window) calls `__cache_wb_inval` (0xa0022ba0) with both arguments unchanged at 0xa001b384 and returns its result. Addresses below 0xffffffff80000000, in 0xffffffffa0000000 to 0xffffffffbfffffff, or from 0xffffffffff600000 up, return at once with nothing done, as does a zero length. For the mapped range from 0xffffffffc0000000 up to 0xffffffffff5fffff it loops per page: it calls `probe_tlb` (0xa00247dc) with the address and 0, uses the result unchecked as the index for `get_tlblo0` (0xa0024670) when bit 12 of the address is clear or `get_tlblo1` (0xa00246b8) when it is set, shifts the EntryLo value right by 6 (sign-extending the low word) to get the frame number, forms the chunk length as the bytes left in the 4096-byte page or the remaining length if that is smaller, and calls `__cache_wb_inval` on the cached address 0xffffffff80000000 ORed with ((frame number shifted left 12) plus the page offset) masked to 0x3fffff. It subtracts the chunk from the length, moves the address on and stops when the length reaches zero. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa001b18c in `_set_memory` (0xa001b140), after the store of a 1, 2, 4 or 8-byte value, with the address and width.
- `_gfxReopen` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: (re)initialises the graphics console and counts opens, a 248-byte static routine at 0xa002ab20. Established.
  The first argument is the I/O record and the second says whether to run the hardware set-up. If the word at offset 4 of the record (the unit) is non-zero it stores 20 at offset 32 and returns 20. Otherwise (second argument non-zero, or count zero) it stores zero at 0xa0059724 (the flag `_gfxwrite` and `_gfxioctl` test), calls the routine at 0xa002a898 with its second argument (`htp_gfx_init` in the debug information) and compares the result with -1. On -1 it decrements the open count, sets the environment variable `gfx` (string at 0xa00376c0) to `dead` (0xa00376c8) with `syssetenv` (0xa002ba40), stores 8 at offset 32 of the record and returns 8. Otherwise it calls `getenv` (0xa002ba5c) for `gfx`, sets it to `alive` (0xa00376d0) first when it was unset, sets it to `alive` again with the store of 1 to 0xa0059724 in that call's delay slot, increments the open count and returns 0. The callers are the `jal` at 0xa002ab0c in `_gfxopen` (second argument 0) and the `jal` at 0xa002ad20 in `_gfxioctl` (second argument 1).
- `_gfxwrite` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes a buffer to the graphics console, an 88-byte static routine at 0xa002ac3c. Established.
  The argument is the I/O record. When the flag word at 0xa0059724 is zero it stores 20 at offset 32 of the record and returns 20. - `_gfxioctl` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: handles control requests for the graphics console, a 156-byte static routine at 0xa002ac94. Established.

- `__dprintf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints a debugging message only when verbose mode is on, a 40-byte static routine at 0xa001d148. Established.
  It loads the word at 0xa0059744 (the `Verbose` word that `_init_saio` fills from the environment) and, when it is non-zero, calls `printf` (0xa002e620) with the arguments it received (it changes none of the argument registers), then returns; when it is zero it returns at once without calling. Six `jal` sites call it: 0xa001cafc, 0xa001cc70, 0xa001cc84 and 0xa001cc98 (inside `getpkt`, 0xa001ca30), 0xa001cd80 (inside the packet-reading helper at 0xa001ccc8) and 0xa001ce74 (inside `putpkt`, 0xa001cdf0), all in the serial protocol code.

  The argument is a tree node. The first count is 1 when the word at offset 0 of the node is 1 and the word at offset 4 is 1, and 0 otherwise. It then takes `GetChild` (0xa00241c8) of the node and, for each child in turn, adds the result of a recursive call and moves on with `GetPeer` (0xa00241c0) until that returns zero. It returns the sum. The callers are the recursive `jal` at 0xa0031668 and the `jal` at 0xa00316e0 in `_get_numcpus` (0xa00316b0), which passes `GetChild` of the null node.
- `_delete_str` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: removes the first `name=value` string whose name matches, from a string table, a 260-byte static routine at 0xa002b424. Established.
  The arguments are the name (a0, held in `s3`), the table (a1, `s2`) and an optional name buffer (a2, `s4`). It takes `strlen` (0xa00309ac) of the name, then scans the word-sized pointers from the start of the table up to the count held in the word at offset 1540 of the table, comparing each string's first name-length bytes with `strncasecmp` (0xa0030224) and requiring a `=` (61) right after. It then moves each later pointer one place down, including the zero pointer that ends the list, and decrements the count at offset 1540. No match means no change. It returns no value. The routine at 0xa002b408 (`delete_str`) calls it with a zero buffer (the `jal` at 0xa002b410) and `replace_str` (0xa002b3b0) calls it at 0xa002b3cc.
- `_clear_cache` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: flushes the data cache for a range of virtual addresses, including mapped kernel addresses, a 292-byte static routine at 0xa001b270. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0, kept in `s0`) and the length (a1, `s1`). An address from 0xffffffff80000000 up to but not including 0xffffffffa0000000 (the cached window) tail-calls into `__cache_wb_inval` (0xa0022ba0) with both arguments unchanged at 0xa001b384, and returns its result. Addresses below 0xffffffff80000000, in 0xffffffffa0000000 to 0xffffffffbfffffff, or from 0xffffffffff600000 up, return at once with nothing done, as does a zero length. For the mapped range from 0xffffffffc0000000 up to 0xffffffffff5fffff it loops per page: it calls `probe_tlb` (0xa00247dc) with the address and 0, uses the result unchecked as the index for `get_tlblo0` (0xa0024670) when bit 12 of the address is clear or `get_tlblo1` (0xa00246b8) when it is set, shifts the EntryLo value right by 6 (sign-extending the low word) to get the frame number, forms the chunk length as the bytes left in the 4096-byte page or the remaining length if that is smaller, and calls `__cache_wb_inval` on the cached address 0xffffffff80000000 ORed with ((frame number shifted left 12) plus the page offset) masked to 0x3fffff. It subtracts the chunk from the length, moves the address on and stops when the length reaches zero. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa001b18c in `_set_memory` (0xa001b140), after the store of a 1, 2, 4 or 8-byte value, with the address and width.
- `DispTag` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints one cache tag line, a 388-byte static routine at 0xa0017ac0. Established.
  The arguments are the cache code (a0, kept in `s0`), the address (a1, `s2`), the slot number (a2, `s1`) and a flag word (a3, `s3`): bit 0 blanks the label, bit 1 is set by callers but never tested here, bit 2 asks for the second way. It calls `_read_tag` (0xa00238dc) with the cache code, the low word of the address sign-extended and a 16-byte buffer on its stack, and keeps the returned check bits for its own return value. It picks the label from `short_tab` (0xa00385c0, indexed by the cache code) or, with bit 0 set, from `blanks` (0xa00385d0 for the primary caches, 0xa00385d4 for cache code 3). It reads `get_prid` (0xa002a6d0) and, when the implementation field (PRId shifted right by 8) is below 35, chooses the field-description table `p_taglo_desc` (0xa003bae0) or, for code 3, `s_taglo_desc` (0xa003bba0); from 35 up it chooses `r4k_p_taglo_desc` (0xa003b738) or `r4k_s_taglo_desc` (0xa003b6c0). It then calls `printf` (0xa002e620) with the format at 0xa0033170 for code 3 (`%s 0x%x (slot %d):<tab>%R`) or the format at 0xa0033188 (the same with two leading spaces) for the others, passing the label, the address, the slot, the first word of the buffer and the table. If bit 2 is set and the implementation field is below 35 again, it calls itself with the same code and slot, the address plus 1 and the flag word 1. It returns the check bits in `v0` on both paths. Sixteen `jal` sites call it: seven in `_caddr` (0xa001709c, 0xa001720c, 0xa0017244, 0xa001727c, 0xa0017420, 0xa0017434, 0xa0017448), seven in `_cidx` (0xa00174f4, 0xa0017508, 0xa001751c, 0xa001758c, 0xa0017714, 0xa0017740, 0xa0017770), one in `_cecc` (0xa0017998) and its own recursion at 0xa0017bac.
- `_cecc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints the ECC check bits of a range of cache lines, a 684-byte static routine at 0xa0017814. Established.
  The arguments are the cache code (a0), the address (a1) and the byte count (a2). An address below 0xffffffffa0000000 and not already cached is ORed with 0x80000000 and cut to 32 bits, one in 0xffffffffa0000000 to 0xffffffffbfffffff has bits 29 and 30 cleared (cached alias), and others are left alone. Code 0 uses the instruction-cache line size and size (0xa005a610, 0xa005a640), code 1 the data-cache pair (0xa005a620, 0xa005a650) and any other code is forced to 3 with the secondary pair (0xa005a630, 0xa005a660); the address is rounded down to a line and the slot is the line's offset within the cache size divided by the line size. A byte count of 1 or less ends the range one line on; otherwise the count is raised by one line when it is not a multiple, masked to whole lines and added to the start, and the end is never less than one line on. It prints the heading `%s ECC checkbits in hex, 0x%x to 0x%x:` (format at 0xa0033130) with the name from `c_str_tab` (0xa00385b0). It then steps 8 bytes at a time: at a line boundary it prints a newline (not for the first line) and bumps the slot, calls `DispTag` (0xa0017ac0) with flag 1 and prints its result as ` %x `; elsewhere it calls `_read_tag` and prints the returned bits as `%x `. After the loop it prints a newline and returns 0. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa0016ee8 in `_c_tags` (0xa0016d78), made for the `cecc` command.
- `_cidx` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: shows cache tags by slot number, a 956-byte static routine at 0xa0017458. Inferred.
  The arguments are the cache code (a0, `s4`), the first slot (a1, `s1`) and the slot count (a2), which is reduced by one when non-zero. The line sizes come from 0xa005a610, 0xa005a620 and 0xa005a630 and the cache sizes from 0xa005a640, 0xa005a650 and 0xa005a660. Codes 0, 1 and 3 turn the first slot into an address from 0xffffffff80000000 (slot times the line size) and the end into the same for slot plus count; they then call `DispTag` (0xa0017ac0) once per slot with flag 0, the address advanced by the line size and the slot number incremented, until the end is passed. Code 5 (address-specified) uses the secondary line size for the slot-to-address step and, per secondary line, calls `DispTag` for the secondary cache, then for each data-cache line in that span (flag 0 for the first, 1 for later), then each instruction-cache line the same way, with the data and instruction slots derived by dividing the address by the cache size and line size. Code 4 shows one address's three tags in the order secondary, data, instruction with flag 0. It returns 0, and its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa0016f7c in `_c_tags` (0xa0016d78), made for the `cidx` command.
- `_caddr` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: shows cache tags for an address range, a 1212-byte static routine at 0xa0016f9c. Inferred.
  The arguments are the cache code (a0, `s3`), the start address (a1) and the byte count (a2). The address is made cached the way `_cecc` does it (0xa0016fc8 to 0xa0017040), and the line sizes and cache sizes are the words at 0xa005a610, 0xa005a620, 0xa005a630, 0xa005a640, 0xa005a650 and 0xa005a660. Code 0, 1 or 3 rounds the address down to a line, finds the slot by dividing the offset in the cache by the line size, takes an end of one line when the count is 1 or less (otherwise the count masked to lines added to the start) and loops `DispTag` (0xa0017ac0) with flag 4, adding the line size and one slot per pass, and stops at once if the end is before the start. Code 5, and code 4 with a count of 2 or more, round the start down to a secondary line, compute the secondary, data and instruction slots, and for each secondary line call `DispTag` for the secondary cache with flag 6, then the data-cache lines of that span (flag 6 for the first, 7 for later ones) and then the instruction-cache lines in the same way. Code 4 with a count of 1 or less shows the secondary, data and instruction tags at the one address with flag 4 each. It returns 0, and its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa0016f68 in `_c_tags` (0xa0016d78), made for the `caddr` command.

# Routines of `symmon.IP32`, one entry each

`symmon.IP32` (`sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`, N32, IRIX 6.5.30) is the stand-alone kernel debugger and monitor. It is not stripped; names and sizes come from its symbol table and addresses are its load addresses. Each routine below was read in full from `objdump -d`, with its strings and globals taken from `.rodata`, `.data`, `.sdata` and `.bss` and its call targets resolved by address. A name followed by an address with no symbol is an unnamed static routine.

- `_ip32_disable_serial_dma` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: clears bit 9 in four 64-bit registers and keeps their old values, a 100-byte routine at address 0xa001140c. Established for the code; that the registers belong to the serial ports' DMA is inferred from the routine's name alone.
  It takes no arguments. It loads the doublewords at 0xbf318000, 0xbf318020, 0xbf31c000 and 0xbf31c020 in that order, stores them as four consecutive doublewords from 0xa003c920 (the start of `.bss`), then writes each back with every bit set except bit 9 (the constant -513), in the same address order. The last write is in the delay slot of the return and `v0` is left holding 0xbf31c020.

- `dumpPrimaryCache` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: walks every index of the primary data cache and dumps it, a 100-byte routine at address 0xa0018200. Established.
  It calls `dCacheSize` (0xa0023dd8), shifts the size right by 6 and, for each value from 0 up to that count (a count of zero or less skips the loop), calls `dumpPrimaryDataLine` (0xa001812c) twice: with the second argument 0 and then 1, the third argument being 1 both times. It returns 0.

- `dumpSecondaryCache` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the same walk over the secondary cache, a 100-byte routine at address 0xa0018264. Established.
  The count is the result of `sCacheSize` (0xa0023d7c) shifted right by 7. Each index goes to `dumpSecondaryLine` (0xa0017fb0) twice, with the second argument 0 then 1 and the third argument 1. The routine returns 0, including for an empty count.

- `isatty` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: decides whether a descriptor is a terminal-like device, a 100-byte routine at address 0xa0026ec0. Established; reading the five type numbers as console devices is inferred.
  It passes its argument unchanged to `get_eiob` (0xa0026e48) and returns 0 when that finds nothing. Otherwise it follows the pointer at descriptor offset 52 to a record, reads the word at record offset 4 and returns 1 when it equals 18, 35, 20, 33 or 32 (tested in that order) and 0 for any other value.

- `_read_tag` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the cache tag at an index with the CPU's tag-load operation, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa00238dc. Established.
  Its first argument selects the cache (0 instruction, 1 primary data, any other value secondary), its second the address operand and its third the place to store the result. After a `sync` it issues `cache` with operation 0x4, 0x5 or 0x7 respectively (the three index-load-tag encodings), reads CP0 TagLo and stores that one 32-bit word through the third argument. TagHi is not read. It returns 0.

- `_write_tag` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes a cache tag at an index, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa002393c. Established.
  It loads the word at the third argument into CP0 TagLo, then, after a `sync`, issues `cache` with operation 0x8 for first argument 0, 0x9 for 1 and 0xb for any other value (the index-store-tag encodings of the instruction, data and secondary caches) on the address in the second argument. TagHi is left as it was. It returns nothing.

- `iLine` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: copies one primary instruction-cache line and its tag into a buffer, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa0023df4. Established; the data-read meaning of operation 0x18 is inferred.
  The first argument is the line's address and the second the buffer. It issues `cache` 0x4 (index load tag), joins TagHi above TagLo and stores that doubleword at buffer offset 0. It then makes sixteen passes, advancing the address by 4 each time until it has moved 64 bytes: each issues `cache` 0x18 on the address, stores TagHi above TagLo as a doubleword from buffer offset 8 in steps of 8, and stores the CP0 ECC byte from offset 136 in steps of 1.

- `_cont` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: resumes the program that is being debugged, a 92-byte routine at address 0xa0012364. Established.
  With any first argument but 1 it returns 1 at once. With 1 it calls `setjmp` (0xa00302dc) on the buffer whose address is the word at 0xa0058e50. A non-zero return from `setjmp` makes `_cont` return 0. On the direct return it stores 1 at 0xa0058e54 (in the delay slot of the next call), calls the routine at 0xa00123c0 that follows `_cont` with 0 and 1 as arguments and returns what that routine returns.

- `_init_libsc_private` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: makes sure the library's per-processor area exists, a 92-byte routine at address 0xa00316fc. Established.
  It calls `_get_numcpus` (0xa00316b0). With two or more processors it calls `malloc` for that count times 128 bytes and stores the result in `_libsc_private` (0xa003c910, which starts out holding the address 0xa005a558 in `.bss`). With fewer it leaves that word alone. In both cases a zero word afterwards calls `panic` with "libsc private area neither init'd nor malloc'd." (0xa0037df8); the routine otherwise returns nothing.

- `cpu_makecfgroot` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: creates the root of the machine configuration tree, a 92-byte routine at address 0xa0027b68. Established.
  It calls `AddChild` (0xa0024228) with a null parent, the static component record at 0xa003c100 and a null third argument, and returns the new node. When the node is null it first calls `__assert` (0xa0027fc8) with the strings "root" (0xa0037468) and "IP32.c" (0xa0037470) and the line number 919.
- `idbg_interrupted` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: handles a keyboard interrupt of the debugger by dropping back to its prompt, a 92-byte routine at address 0xa001a1c8. Established.
  It calls `symmon_spl` (0xa0010e6c), loads the pointer at 0xa0001010, calls the routine at 0xa001ad98 with the words at 0xa0058e4c and 0xa0058e00, stores zero at offset 12 of the pointed-to record, prints "Interrupted" (0xa00350a8) with `printf` (0xa002e620), stores zero at 0xa0058e04 and calls `_restart` (0xa00184a0), whose result it returns.

- `probe_tlb` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: asks the TLB whether it holds a translation for an address and address-space number, a 92-byte routine at address 0xa00247dc. Established.
  It clears bit 0 of CP0 Status, forms EntryHi from the first argument with its low 13 bits cleared and the low 8 bits of the second argument, issues `tlbp`, reads CP0 Index into `v0`, then restores EntryHi and Status. The caller receives Index, whose top bit the probe sets when nothing matched.

- `proto_enable` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: turns on the debugger's serial protocol on a descriptor, a 92-byte routine at address 0xa001d170. Established.
  It calls `isatty` (0xa0026ec0) on its argument. For a terminal it calls `ioctl` (0xa0026ba0) with requests 29697 (0x7401) and an argument of 1, then 29698 (0x7402) with 0, and returns the second result. For anything else it prints "not a character device" (0xa0035de8) with `printf` and returns its result.

- `_set_register` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: stores a value in a saved register slot of the debugger, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa001b218. Established.
  The first argument is the register number and the second the 64-bit value. A number of 93 or more calls `_dbg_error` (0xa0018428) with "invalid register number" (0xa0035308); if that returns, the code continues, so the store below still happens (the test is signed, so a negative number passes it without the call). The value goes to the doubleword at the pointer held in 0xa0058e00 plus 8 times the number.

- `close_noncons` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: closes every open descriptor that is not a terminal, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa002b0ac. Established.
  For descriptors 0 to 19 it calls `isatty` (0xa0026ec0) and, for each that answers 0, `Close` (0xa00255a0). It takes no arguments and returns nothing.

- `dumpPrimaryDataLineAddr` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: dumps the primary data-cache line that holds an address, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa00181a8. Established.
  Its arguments are an address, an offset added to the aligned address, and a flag passed on. It sets bit 31 of the address, clears the low 5 bits and keeps the result at stack offset 64, calls `dLine` (0xa0023e54) with the aligned address plus the offset and a stack buffer, then calls the routine at 0xa0017d70 with the buffer, the aligned address and the flag, and returns 0.

- `dumpPrimaryInstLineAddr` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the instruction-cache twin of the data-line dump, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa00180d4. Established.
  The alignment is 64 bytes (the low 6 bits cleared) instead of 32, the line is read with `iLine` (0xa0023df4) into a buffer on the stack, and the printing routine is the one at 0xa0017c44. The arguments and the zero return are as for the data-line routine.

- `findFlashSegment` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: finds the first flash segment with a valid body, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa0029d84. Established.
  It calls `findFlashSegmentHdr` (0xa0029cf4) with its two arguments, then loops: a zero header ends the loop, a header for which `validBody` (0xa0029ea4) answers non-zero ends it, and otherwise it asks `findFlashSegmentHdr` for the next header with the current one as third argument. It returns the last header, or 0.

- `get_watchlo` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the CPU's low watchpoint register where one exists, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa0024b40. Established.
  It reads the implementation field of CP0 PRId (bits 15:8) and returns 0 without touching WatchLo when it is 0x20, 0x21, 0x23 or 0x28. For any other value it returns CP0 WatchLo.

- `get_watchhi` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the high-word twin of the low watchpoint reader, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa0024b98. Established.
  The same implementation numbers (0x20, 0x21, 0x23 and 0x28) return 0; every other part returns CP0 WatchHi.
- `getchar` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads one character from the console, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa002d310. Established.
  It calls `Read` (0xa00251ec) on descriptor 0 for one byte with a count variable on the stack. When `Read` returns 0 and the count is 1 it returns the byte. In every other case it returns the result of `_errgetc` (0xa002a890).

- `putc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes one character to a stream, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa001d208. Established.
  The first argument is the character and the second the descriptor. It stores the byte on the stack and calls `Write` (0xa002545c) for one byte. When `Write` returns 0 and reports a count of 1 it returns the character; otherwise it returns -1.

- `replace_str` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: replaces a string in a buffer, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa002b3b0. Established.
  It clears the first byte of a scratch buffer on the stack and calls the routine at 0xa002b424 with the first argument, the third argument and that buffer. It then calls `new_str2` (0xa002b6d4) with the second and third arguments unchanged and, as its first, the scratch buffer when the routine left it non-empty and the original first argument when it is still empty. It returns the result of `new_str2`.

- `Signal` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: installs a handler for a signal number, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa002495c. Established.
  A number of 0 or less, or 20 or more, returns 7. Otherwise it returns the old entry of the table at 0xa0058c90 (four bytes per number) and stores a new one: a second argument of 0 installs the routine at 0xa00249bc (which calls `EnterInteractiveMode`, 0xa0031758), 1 installs the routine at 0xa00249d8 (a bare return), and any other value is stored as given; the last case is the branch-likely `bnel`.

- `lookup_cmd` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: finds a command by name in a table, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa001b864. Established.
  The first argument is a table of 12-byte entries whose first word points to the entry's name, ending at an entry with a zero name pointer; the second is the name sought. It compares each name with `strcmp` (0xa0030938) and returns the address of the first entry that matches, or 0 at the end of the table.

- `strncat` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: appends at most a given number of bytes of one string to another, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa0030e10. Established.
  It finds the end of the first string, then copies bytes of the second string, stopping after it has copied a terminating zero; if the count runs out first it stores a zero after the last byte. The first byte is copied before the count is looked at, and a zero count then overwrites it with a zero. It returns the first argument.

- `strncpy` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: copies a string and pads it with zeros to a fixed length, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa0030db0. Established.
  A count of zero or less returns the destination at once. Otherwise it copies bytes one at a time up to the count; after a zero byte is stored it fills the remaining positions with zero bytes. A string that fills the count exactly gets no terminator. It returns the destination.

- `_circ_getc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: takes one byte from a circular buffer, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa002840c. Established.
  The argument is the buffer record: the word at offset 4 is the write pointer, the word at offset 8 the read pointer, and the bytes run from offset 12 to offset 1036. When the pointers are equal it calls `panic` (0xa002af8c) with "console getc error" (0xa00375b8). It then returns the byte at the read pointer and advances that pointer, wrapping to offset 12 at offset 1036.

- `_circ_putc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: puts one byte into a circular buffer, an 88-byte routine at address 0xa002846c. Established.
  Its first argument is the byte and the second the same buffer record. It forms the next write position (wrapping at offset 1036 to offset 12); when that equals the read pointer the buffer is full, the byte is dropped and, only if the word `Verbose` (0xa0059744) is non-zero, it calls `_errputs` (0xa002a79c) with "dropped char" (0xa00375d0). Otherwise it stores the byte at the old write position in the delay slot of the branch-likely `bnel` and then updates the write pointer. It returns nothing.

- `GetComponent` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: passes its argument through two helper routines and the first child of the configuration tree, a 96-byte routine at address 0xa0025fe8. Established; the two helper routines at 0xa0025bcc and 0xa0025e60 are other entries' subjects.
  It passes its argument and the address of a flag word (cleared first) to the routine at 0xa0025bcc, calls `GetChild` (0xa00241c8) with 0, and then calls the routine at 0xa0025e60 with that child, the constant 43, 0 and the first routine's result. When the flag word is set it calls `free` (0xa002fd18) on the first routine's result. It returns the result of the routine at 0xa0025e60.

- `kpprintf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: calls `printf` under the debugger's own global pointer, a 68-byte routine at 0xa001101c (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It stores `gp` and `ra` as doublewords at 0xa0037e30 and 0xa0037e38 (the pair is formed with `lui` and `addiu` at 0xa001101c to 0xa0011034), loads `gp` from the doubleword at the per-CPU block word `gen_pda_tab` +88 (address 0xa0058e10, through `k1` = 0xa0058db8) in the delay slot of `jal printf` (0xa002e620) at 0xa001103c, and then reloads `ra` and `gp` from the two saved slots. Its own arguments reach `printf` untouched, and the single exit at 0xa0011058 returns what `printf` returned, with the `gp` reload in the delay slot. No `jal` targets it. Its address is built at 0xa001a370 and 0xa001ac30 and stored at offset 12 of a record in each case (0xa001a378 and 0xa001ac38).
- `showException` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: formats four words as hexadecimal text and prints them, a 68-byte routine at 0xa0027f84 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It opens a 96-byte frame and calls `sprintf` (0xa002e920) at 0xa0027fac with the frame as destination, the format "%08x %08x %08x %08x\n" (0xa0037490) and its four arguments in order; the first argument is held in `v0` and passed as the third argument, the second is held in `at` and passed as the fourth, and the third and fourth go on as the fifth and sixth by the `move`s at 0xa0027f88 and 0xa0027f84. It then calls `_errputs` (0xa002a79c) on the frame and returns that call's result at 0xa0027fc0. No `jal` or `j` targets it.
- `sn0_getcpuid` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: asks a function table in low memory for the current processor number, a 68-byte routine at 0xa0030f84 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; that the word at 0xa0001024 is a table size or count is inferred from its use here.
  It loads the word at 0xa0001024 (`lui v0,0xa000` and `lw` at +4132) and compares it unsigned against 65 (`sltiu` at 0xa0030f90). When it is 64 or less the routine returns -1 (the `li a2,-1` at 0xa0030f9c, copied to `v0` at 0xa0030fa0). Otherwise it loads the pointer at 0xa0001028, loads a function pointer from offset 64 of the record it points to, calls it with `jalr` at 0xa0030fb4 with no arguments, and returns the callee's `v0` (through `a2`). Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa002b2cc. The name says SN0 although this is the O2 debugger, so the routine is shared code; I did not look for what writes 0xa0001024.
- `validBody` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: sums the words of a block and reports whether the sum is zero, a 68-byte routine at 0xa0029ea4 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; that the block is a loaded image record is inferred.
  The argument in a0 is the block address. The sum starts at a0 +64 and runs a word at a time with a 32-bit add to an end address formed as a0 plus the word at a0 +12, rounded up to a multiple of 4 (`addiu 3`, `and` with -4, at 0xa0029eb4 and 0xa0029eb8). It stops when the pointer equals the end; the loop test is a `bne`, so a length that rounds to less than 64 is not handled. When start and end are equal at the outset (lengths 61 to 64 for a word-aligned block) no word is read and the sum is zero. The return value at 0xa0029ed8 is `sltiu v0,a2,1`: 1 when the sum is zero and 0 otherwise. It has no callee and one caller, the `jal` at 0xa0029da4.
- `_ip32_restore_serial_dma` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes four saved doublewords back to the serial-port registers, a 64-byte routine at 0xa0011470 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It loads the four doublewords at 0xa003c920, 0xa003c928, 0xa003c930 and 0xa003c938 (the area `_ip32_disable_serial_dma` fills) and stores them, in this order, to 0xbf318000, 0xbf318020, 0xbf31c000 and 0xbf31c020; the last store is in the return's delay slot at 0xa00114ac. It returns no value and calls nothing. The two `jal` sites are 0xa00109b0 and 0xa0010cf4. That the four registers belong to the serial ports' DMA is the earlier entry's inference from the routine's name and is not tested here.
- `_restart` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: abandons the current command and jumps back to the debugger's saved return point, a 64-byte routine at 0xa00184a0 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls `symmon_spl` (0xa0010e6c), then clears the flag word at `gen_pda_tab` +128 (address 0xa0058e38, the store is in the delay slot of the next `jal` at 0xa00184b8) and calls the unnamed routine at 0xa0018510, which closes every descriptor in a table whose count word is at 0xa003ccd0, from the last entry to the first. It then calls `longjmp` (0xa0030318) with the jump buffer pointer at `gen_pda_tab` +140 (address 0xa0058e44) and the value 1 (`li a1,1` at 0xa00184c0). `longjmp` does not return, so the epilogue at 0xa00184d4 is not reached in practice. Five `jal` sites call it: 0xa0012b1c, 0xa0018414 (`_fatal_error`), 0xa0018474, 0xa0019fec and 0xa001a210.
- `clear_nofault` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: clears two fault-recovery words in the current processor's block, a 64-byte routine at 0xa001a04c (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls `cpuid` (0xa0020c5c), keeps the result in `s8`, calls `_init_alarm` (0xa0024d80), and shifts the processor number left by 9 (blocks are 512 bytes apart). It stores zero to the words at +52 and +76 of that block, counted from `gen_pda_tab` (0xa0058db8). The word at +76 is the recovery-buffer pointer that `_PrintInstruction` arms, and the store to it is in the return's delay slot at 0xa001a088. It saves and restores `s8` and leaves the shifted processor number in `v0`, which is not a result. The one caller is the `jal` at 0xa0019fcc.
- `cpufreq` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: converts the processor clock reading to whole megahertz, a 64-byte routine at 0xa00281dc (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls `cpu_get_freq` (0xa0027690), doubles the result, adds 500000 and divides by 1000000 with a signed `div` (constants formed by the `lui` and `ori` pairs at 0xa00281f0 to 0xa00281fc); the quotient is read with `mflo` at 0xa0028210 and returned. The `teq` in the return's delay slot traps only on a zero divisor, which the constant rules out. No `jal` targets it; the word at 0xa003bfe8 holds its address.

- `led_on` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: rewrites the two status-light bits of the doubleword at 0xbf310008, a 64-byte routine at 0xa0028600 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; that bits 5 and 4 drive status lights is an inference from the routine's purpose in the debugger.
  The argument in a0 is masked with 0x30 (`andi` at 0xa0028618). It loads the doubleword at 0xbf310008, sets bits 5 and 4 (`ori` 0x30), exclusive-ors the masked argument back in and stores the result with `sd` at 0xa0028628, so a bit named in the argument ends up clear and the other of the two ends up set. It saves `t0`, `at` and `t1` in a 64-byte frame and reloads them before the return. The `addiu sp,sp,64` that should drop the frame (0xa002863c) sits after the return's delay slot and is never reached, so the routine gives back a stack pointer 64 bytes below the one it received. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `led_off` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: sets bits in the doubleword at 0xbf310008, a 56-byte routine at 0xa0028640 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; that this switches a light off is an inference from `led_on` clearing the same two bits.
  It ors the whole of a0, unmasked, into the doubleword it loads from 0xbf310008 and stores the result back with `sd` at 0xa0028660. It saves and reloads `t0`, `at` and `t1` around the access in a 64-byte frame, and, as in `led_on`, the `addiu sp,sp,64` at 0xa0028674 follows the return's delay slot and is never reached. It calls nothing and returns no value. I found no direct reference to it.
- `lookup_regname` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads a debugger register by name, a 64-byte routine at 0xa0015348 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  The first argument is the name and the second the address that receives the value. It saves the second argument at 0(sp) and calls the unnamed routine at 0xa00152e0 with the name; that routine walks the table of 12-byte records that starts at 0xa0037f38 (a name pointer at +0, a register number at +4), comparing each name with `strcmp` (0xa0030938) until the pointer is zero, and returns the record or 0. When it finds none, `lookup_regname` returns 1 (the `li a3,1` at 0xa001535c, copied to `v0`). Otherwise it passes the number from record +4 to `_get_register` (0xa001b1c8), stores the 64-bit result through the saved pointer (the `sd` at 0xa0015384) and returns 0. Its one caller is the `jal` at 0xa001c684.
- `sCacheSize` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: sizes the secondary cache from CP0 Config and sets its line constants, a 64-byte routine at 0xa0023d7c (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; reading Config bits 18:16 as a secondary-cache size field is inferred, since the routine does not name a processor.
  It reads Config, masks it with 0x70000, shifts right 16 and adds 19 (`daddi` at 0xa0023d8c). It stores 64 to the word `_scache_linesize` (0xa005a630) and then 63 to `_scache_linemask` (0xa005a700). The return value is 1 shifted left by that sum (`dsllv` in the delay slot at 0xa0023db8), 512 KiB for a field of 0, doubling for each step. It calls nothing. The `jal` sites are 0xa0017ebc, 0xa001826c, 0xa00234f0 and 0xa0023ecc.
- `strcmp` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: compares two NUL-terminated strings, a 64-byte routine at 0xa0030938 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It loads one byte from each argument with `lbu`; when they differ it returns the first byte minus the second (`subu` at 0xa0030964), which is positive when the first string is greater. When they match it reloads the first byte and returns 0 if that byte is zero (the exit at 0xa0030970); otherwise it advances both pointers and repeats. It calls nothing. It has 20 `jal` sites, the first at 0xa00136f0 and the last at 0xa00280fc.
- `ticksper1024inst` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: times a counted loop twice against the CRIME timer and returns the second reading, a 64-byte routine at 0xa0028678 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It points `t0` at the doubleword at CRIME +0x38 (0xb4000038) and loads `t3` with 0xa002868c (the `addiu` at 0xa0028688, in the delay slot of the `b` at 0xa0028684). At 0xa0028694 it sets a counter of 512, reads the timer into `t2`, spins on `bgtz` with the decrement in its delay slot (the pair runs 513 times, the counter reaching -1), reads the timer into `v0` and jumps through `t3` with the 64-bit subtract `v0 - t2` in the delay slot (0xa00286ac). The first pass lands at 0xa002868c, which reloads `t3` with 0xa00286b0 and falls into the same loop; the second pass lands at 0xa00286b0, `jr ra`. The first pass therefore warms the instruction cache and the returned value, the timer ticks of the second pass, comes from the second. It has no caller I could find, and it differs from `_ticksper1024inst`, which loads cache lines instead of repeating and counts from 511.
- `setjmp` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: saves the callee-saved registers into a buffer and returns 0, a 60-byte routine at 0xa00302dc (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It stores doublewords through a0 in this order: `ra` at 0, `sp` at 8, `s8` at 16, `s0` to `s7` at 24, 32, 40, 48, 56, 64, 72 and 80, `s8` again at 88 and `t9` at 96. It does not save `gp`. `v0` is cleared in the return's delay slot (0xa0030314). It calls nothing. The `longjmp` at 0xa0030318, which follows it, is outside this entry. Fourteen `jal` sites call it, the first at 0xa0011170 and the last at 0xa001d38c.
- `GetConfigurationData` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: copies a configuration component's data block to the caller's buffer, a 60-byte routine at 0xa00241ec (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; the role follows the routine's name and its place in the firmware-interface table.
  The first argument is the destination and the second the component record. It loads the word at record +24 (the length) and, when it is zero, returns 7 (the `li v0,7` at 0xa002420c, in the delay slot of the `b` at 0xa0024208). It then loads the word at record +52 (the data pointer) and returns 7 in the same way when that is zero. Otherwise it calls `bcopy` (0xa002ca40) with the data pointer as source, the original first argument as destination and the length, and returns 0. The word at 0xa003bf58 holds the routine's address; no `jal` targets it.
- `__assert` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: formats an assertion message and stops the debugger, a 60-byte routine at 0xa0027fc8 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  The arguments are the expression text (a0), the file name (a1) and the line number (a2). It builds the message in a 144-byte frame with `sprintf` (0xa002e920), format "assertion failure (%s) in file %s at line %d\n" (0xa00374a8), passing the expression as the third argument, the file as the fourth and the line as the fifth, then calls `panic` (0xa002af8c) on the buffer. `panic` is not expected to return; the epilogue at 0xa0027ff8 would return its result. Sixteen `jal` sites call it, the first at 0xa00201b8 and the last at 0xa0028140.
- `__close_fds` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: closes the first 20 file descriptors, a 60-byte routine at 0xa002aec0 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It counts `s0` from 0 and calls `Close` (0xa00255a0) with it at 0xa002aed8, stopping when the count reaches 20 (the `bne` with `s1` at 0xa002aee4), so descriptors 0 to 19 are closed in ascending order. The `ld ra` in that branch's delay slot runs on every pass. It returns whatever the last `Close` left in `v0`. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `_errputc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes one error-message character to the graphics console or the serial port, a 60-byte routine at 0xa002a854 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It stores the byte at 23(sp) and calls `console_is_gfx` (0xa0026f24). When that returns zero it calls `cpu_errputc` (0xa0027294) with the byte; otherwise it calls `txPrint` (0xa002aa90) with the address of the stored byte. The `li a1,1` in the delay slot at 0xa002a868 gives both calls a second argument of 1. The routine returns what the callee left in `v0`. The `jal` sites are 0xa002a7f0, 0xa002a844, 0xa002de3c, 0xa002de58 and 0xa002e128.
- `cpu_get_eaddr` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: fetches the board's Ethernet address through the DS2502 reader, a 60-byte routine at 0xa0027314 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; the role follows the callee's name and the use of the argument as the receiving buffer is inferred.
  It saves a0, loads the word `_prom` (0xa005a3e0) and, when it is zero, calls `run_cached` (0xa00114b0) before reloading a0 from the stack. `run_cached` is `jr ra` with a `nop`, so that call does nothing in this image. It then calls `ds2502_get_eaddr` (0xa0028de4) with a0 and returns that call's result. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `prcuroff` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints the current position of an open file, a 60-byte routine at 0xa002b360 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; that `Seek` mode 1 is a relative seek is inferred from the ARC firmware call of that name.
  It zeroes the two words at 0(sp) and 4(sp) and calls `Seek` (0xa002564c) with its argument unchanged, the address of that pair and the mode 1. It then calls `printf` (0xa002e620) with the format "offset=0x%x 0x%x\n" (0xa0037730), the word at 0(sp) and the word at 4(sp), and returns that result. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `proto_disable` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: sends two control requests to a descriptor, a 60-byte routine at 0xa001d1cc (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; the role is inferred.
  It calls `ioctl` (0xa0026ba0) with the descriptor, request 0x7401 and argument 0, then again with request 0x7402 and argument 0, and returns the second result. The routine before it calls the same two requests with an argument of 1 for the first; `edit_gets` uses request 0x7401 with 1 to put the console in raw mode, so this routine is read as undoing that. The `jal` sites are 0xa001d3fc and 0xa001d44c.
- `psignal` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: marks a signal pending, a 60-byte routine at 0xa00249e0 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  The signal number in a0 must be from 1 to 19; otherwise the routine returns 7 (the `li v0,7` in the delay slot at 0xa00249e4 runs on entry, and the two range branches leave it). It sets bit (number - 1) in the word at 0xa0058ce0 with `sllv` and `or` and returns 0. The second argument is not read. The word sits just past the 19-word handler table that `_init_signals` fills. The `jal` sites are 0xa0024e40, 0xa0028274 and 0xa0028294.
- `set_watch` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: programs the CP0 watch registers, a 60-byte routine at 0xa002ae84 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  The address (a0) is masked with -8 and ored with the second argument (the `or` in the delay slot at 0xa002ae9c), and the result goes to `set_watchlo` (0xa0024bf0). The routine then calls `set_watchhi` (0xa0024c70) with 0 and returns what `set_watchlo` returned (kept in `s8`). I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `symmon_nmi` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the NMI stub of the exception entry, a 60-byte routine at 0xa00103e0 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It writes zero to Status (`li k0,0` and `mtc0` at 0xa00103e4), loads the register-record pointer from `gen_pda_tab` +72 (`k1` is 0xa0058db8), stores `at` at record +8, stores 6 to the word at `gen_pda_tab` +136 and 1 to the word at +128 (the last store is in the delay slot of the `j` to `exception` at 0xa0010414). The routine never returns. The routine at 0xa001bb68 (`_dbgmon`) forms its address at 0xa001bba0 and stores it to the word at 0x80000408 (the `sw` at 0xa001bbd4, with `v0` holding 0x80000000).
- `_init_signals` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: resets the debugger's signal table, a 56-byte routine at 0xa0024924 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It stores the address 0xa00249bc into 19 consecutive words from 0xa0058c90 (the store is in the branch's delay slot at 0xa0024944, so the walk ends after the word at 0xa0058cd8), then stores zero to the word at 0xa0058ce0 (the pending-signal word `psignal` sets) and finally replaces the second table word, at 0xa0058c94, with 0xa00249d8, a `jr ra` and `nop`. The word it installs points at a short routine whose only call is to 0xa0031758, the symbol `EnterInteractiveMode`. It returns no value; the one `jal` is at 0xa0026774.
- `addulongtolarge` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: adds an unsigned 32-bit value to a 64-bit number held as two words, a 56-byte routine at 0xa0031140 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It widens a0 into a 16-byte stack record with `ulongtolarge` (0xa0031194) and calls `addlarge` (0xa00310b4) with that record, the second argument and the second argument again as the destination, so the sum replaces the value at a1. `addlarge` adds the word at +4 of each operand, carries into the sum of the words at +0 and returns 1 when a signed overflow occurs and 0 otherwise. The routine returns that result. The `jal` sites are 0xa0025384, 0xa0025418 and 0xa0025578.
- `vflush` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes back and invalidates the cache lines of a cached address range, a 56-byte routine at 0xa0026f70 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; the role follows the callee's name.
  It compares a0 as an unsigned 64-bit value against 0xffffffff80000000 and 0xffffffffa0000000 (the `lui` constants at 0xa0026f70 and 0xa0026f80, sign-extended) and calls `__cache_wb_inval` (0xa0022ba0) only when the address is at or above the first and below the second. The call passes a0 and the caller's a1 on unchanged. For any other address it returns at once. The `addiu sp,sp,-16` sits in the delay slot of the first branch, so the frame is balanced on every path. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `_r4600sc_limit` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: computes a length from two addresses, a 52-byte routine at 0xa00219d8 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; the use as a cache-operation length limit is inferred.
  With a0 and a1 as start and end, it shifts both right by 12. When the results are equal it returns (a1 - a0) with the low five bits kept (`andi` 0x1f at 0xa00219f0); otherwise it returns the distance from a0 to the next 4096-byte boundary, `((a0 + 4096) >> 12 << 12) - a0`, with the low five bits kept. Both exits are `jr t9`, not `jr ra`, so a caller must have left its return address in `t9`. I found no direct branch, `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `GetMemoryDescriptor` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: steps from one memory descriptor to the next, a 52-byte routine at 0xa0029294 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; reading the word at +16 as the link to the next descriptor and the word at 0xa00592b0 as the list head is inferred from the ARC firmware call of that name.
  A zero argument returns the word at 0xa00592b0. Otherwise it loads the word at +12 of the argument and compares it with 0x4b45565e (`lui` and `ori` at 0xa0029298 and 0xa00292a0); the branch-likely `beql` at 0xa00292a4 runs its delay slot, `lw v0,16(a0)`, only when they are equal, and the routine then returns the word at +16. When they differ it returns 0. The word at 0xa003bf70 holds its address.
- `_doadump` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: runs the kernel-dump command, a 52-byte routine at 0xa001bb34 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It builds a two-word argument vector on the stack, the address of the string "doadump" (0xa0035460) at 0(sp) and zero at 4(sp), and calls `_kpcheck` (0xa001a5b8) with 1, that vector and 1 (the `li` instructions at 0xa001bb34 and 0xa001bb38). It returns `_kpcheck`'s result. The word at 0xa0039018 holds its address, beside the pointer to the string "doadump" at 0xa0039014 and one to "kernel dump:\tdoadump" (0xa0035650) at 0xa003901c. No `jal` targets it.
- `_fatal_error` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reports an internal debugger error and restarts the command loop, a 52-byte routine at 0xa00183f4 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls `printf` (0xa002e620) with "DEBUGGER ERROR detected in %s\n" (0xa00336e0) and its own argument. In the delay slot of that call (0xa0018410) it stores zero to the word at `gen_pda_tab` +76 (0xa0058e04), the recovery-buffer pointer, so the clear happens before `printf` runs. It then calls `_restart` (0xa00184a0), which does not return. Nine `jal` sites call it, the first at 0xa001263c and the last at 0xa001d930.
- `_get_iobbuf` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: allocates a 1024-byte I/O buffer or panics, a 52-byte routine at 0xa0026b50 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls `dmabuf_malloc` (0xa002ffc4) with 1024 and returns that pointer in `v0` when it is not zero. A zero result calls `panic` (0xa002af8c) with the string "out of io buffers" (0xa0037070). `panic` does not return, and the epilogue would return its result. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `cpu_acpanic` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints that the configuration tree could not be built, a 52-byte routine at 0xa00272b0 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls `printf` (0xa002e620) with "Cannot malloc %s component of config tree.\n" (0xa0037128) and its own argument as the component name, then with "Incomplete config tree -- cannot continue.\n" (0xa0037158), and returns the second call's result. It does not stop the debugger itself. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `delay` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: waits a number of time units by calling `us_delay`, a 52-byte routine at 0xa00281a8 (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; the unit of the wait is inferred from the callee's name.
  It multiplies a0 by 10000 with shifts and adds (the product 625 times the argument, then shifted left by 4 in the delay slot at 0xa00281cc) and calls `us_delay` (0xa0029048) with it, returning that result. The unit of `us_delay`'s argument is not stated by this routine, so the length of one unit of `delay` is not given here. I found no `jal` or address constant that reaches it.
- `get_cpu_rb` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: finds the restart block of one processor in a linked list, a 52-byte routine at 0xa00312dc (6.5.30, N32). Established for the code; that the blocks are processor restart blocks is inferred, because the two field offsets it uses fit the published firmware restart-block layout.
  It loads the list head from the word at 0xa000100c and returns 0 for an empty list, through the branch-likely beqzl at 0xa00312e4, whose delay slot clears the result only when the branch is taken.
  Each block is tested by comparing the word at +24 with the argument in a0 (the plain beq at 0xa00312f0). A match returns the block's address. A mismatch moves to the block at the word at +12 through the branch-likely bnezl at 0xa00312fc, and the end of the list returns 0. The one caller is the `jal` at 0xa00311d0.
- `mpsetup` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: runs the debugger's four start-up steps, a 52-byte routine at 0xa001be20 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It calls, with no arguments and in this order, `cpu_probe` (0xa0011404), `init_symmon_gpda` (0xa001b394), `_hook_exceptions` (0xa0029a60) and `_save_exceptions` (0xa00297d4), and returns the last one's result. The one caller is the `jal` at 0xa00102f4.
- `strcpy` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: copies a NUL-terminated string, a 52-byte routine at 0xa0030978 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  It keeps the destination in `v0`, copies the first byte in the delay slot of the zero test at 0xa003098c (so an empty string still stores its terminator), then copies each following byte in the delay slot of the loop branch at 0xa003099c until it has copied the zero byte. It returns the destination address and calls nothing. It has 11 `jal` sites, the first at 0xa001b434 and the last at 0xa0030a14.

## Stand-alone debugger (`sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`)

Routines of the stand-alone kernel debugger, read from the 6.5.30 image (an N32 executable linked at 0xa0010200, with a full symbol table and DWARF but no `.mdebug` section; addresses are kseg1). Arguments are named by register: a0 first, a1 second.

- `_PrintInstruction` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the one-instruction disassembler, a 6272-byte routine at address 0xa0018650. Inferred.
  The arguments are the instruction address (a0), a register-name style (a1, any non-zero value selects the second of two 32-entry name tables at 0xa0038e08) and a flag (a2) that asks for the registers the instruction uses to be listed after it.
  It first asks `fetch_kname` (0xa001c08c) for a symbol name for the address and keeps the result in the word at 0xa003cce8; it prints either `[name+0xOFFSET, 0xADDR]:` (offset taken from the per-CPU block word at `gen_pda_tab`+168 when that is non-zero), `[name, 0xADDR]:` or just `0xADDR:`, each followed by a tab.
  It arms a recovery buffer at 0xa003ccf0 in the per-CPU block word at `gen_pda_tab`+76 (`setjmp`), reads the word with `getinst` (0xa0012918) and clears that block word again; if the read faults, control returns to the `setjmp` with a non-zero value, `symmon_spl` runs, `inaccessible` is printed and the routine returns 4.
  With a2 zero it also prints the raw word as eight hex digits (a helper at 0xa0019ed0) and a tab.
  It then switches on the major opcode in bits 31:26 of the word. The SPECIAL group (opcode 0) prints a literal `nop` for a zero word, `nada` or `move` for an add or or with a zero second source, `ssnop` for the sll encoding with shift amount 1, and otherwise the mnemonic from a 64-entry pointer table at 0xa0038748 followed by operands chosen by function code; the REGIMM group (opcode 1) uses the table at 0xa0038848; the coprocessor 0 group (0x10) prints `mfc0`, `mtc0`, `cfc0`, `ctc0`, `bc0` and the TLB and ERET functions by name (a `d` is printed before the mnemonic for the doubleword move forms), the coprocessor 1 group (0x11) the floating-point moves, `bc1` with an optional condition-code field, and the arithmetic, compare and convert functions with their `.s`/`.d`/`.w`/`.l` suffix, the coprocessor 2 group (0x12) the generic `mfc`/`mtc`/`cfc`/`ctc`/`bc` forms with the coprocessor number, and opcode 0x13 the indexed floating-point loads, stores and multiply-add forms. Cache operations (opcode 0x2f) print the operation name and the cache (`Index_Invalidate`, `Hit_WriteBack_Invalidate`, `Create_Dirty_Exclusive`, `Barrier` and the others) beside the base register; loads, stores and prefetch print `0xOFFSET(reg)`. Jumps (opcodes 2 and 3) print the target address and a symbol for it; conditional branches print the target computed from the 16-bit offset, also with a symbol, honouring the `abs_adrs` environment variable, which switches the symbol form to `name+0xOFFSET,0xADDR`.
  When the flag a2 is set it appends, after a tab and `<`, each register the instruction read or wrote, as `name=0xVALUE` with the value taken from the saved register block of the current CPU (`gen_pda_tab`+72 points at it); `<0x...>` after a load or store shows the computed effective address. The register list is built by a helper at 0xa0018564 that records each register number once in a six-entry list and returns the name from the table.
  It ends with a newline. For a branch or jump (the conditional REGIMM forms, opcodes 2 to 7 and 0x14 to 0x17, the coprocessor branches `bc0`, `bc1` and `bc2`, `jr` and `jalr`) it calls itself on the delay-slot word (address plus 4) and returns 8; for every other instruction it returns 4.
- `_dbxdump` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the dbx-style `address/format` memory dump command, an 1864-byte routine at address 0xa0016420. Inferred.
  The argument is the command line (a0). It scans for a `/`; with none, or an empty line, it returns 1 so the caller tries other command forms. A leading `*` means indirect (the value read from the address is used as the address) and a leading `&` is skipped so symbols named like commands can be dumped. The text before the slash is parsed with `parse_sym_range` (0xa001c428, size mode 4); a failed parse returns 0.
  The text after the slash is an optional decimal count followed by a format letter. A letter is remembered in two bytes at 0xa003ccb0 and 0xa003ccb1 (the second holds the following character) so a later command with no letter repeats the last format; a count from a symbol range overrides the typed count, and a count of zero becomes 1.
  Formats: `d` and `x` print halfwords (8 per line, decimal and hex), `o` halfwords in octal, `D`, `X` and `O` words (4 per line), `XX` doublewords as 16 hex digits (2 per line), `b` bytes in octal behind a backslash (8 per line), `c` bytes as a quoted character or `?` (16 per line), `s` a NUL-terminated string with unprintable bytes shown as `.`, `i` or `I` one instruction per call through `_PrintInstruction` (a branch consumes two instructions, so the remaining count drops by two); `f` and `g` print `floating point formats not supported.` and return 0; any other letter returns 1. The start address is aligned down to the unit size for the halfword, word and doubleword formats.
  When the address lies above 0xbfffffff and below 0xff600000 it probes each 4 KiB page the dump will touch with `probe_tlb` and calls `map_k2seg_addr` for each page the probe reports as unmapped. Memory is read through `_get_memory` with the unit size, not by direct load, and each line begins with the address as `%#08Ix:`, except that the `i` format omits it (the `I` format prints it).
  It returns 0 after dumping and 1 for a malformed command.
- `edit_gets` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the line editor behind the command prompt, an 1856-byte routine at address 0xa002d610. Inferred.
  The argument is a history record (a0): ten 256-byte lines starting at the record, with the index of the current line in the word at record +0xa00. It switches the console to raw mode (`ioctl` with request 0x7401 and 1), then reads characters with `getc` until a newline completes a line, and finally restores cooked mode (`ioctl` 0x7401 with 0). The dispatch is a jump through a 129-entry word table at 0xa003782c, indexed by the character plus 1 (so end-of-file, -1, is entry 0); characters above 127 take the default insert path.
  Control characters: ^A moves to the line start (or, when the word at 0xa0001010 is non-zero and the debugger is not already running, enters the debugger through `exec_brkpt` with the string `ring`), ^B and ^F move one position, ^E moves to the end, ^D, ^X and end-of-file delete the character under the cursor or, on an empty line, enter the PROM monitor (`EnterInteractiveMode`, or print `PROM transfer vector not available.` when the vector is missing), backspace and DEL delete behind the cursor, ^K kills to the end, ^U clears the whole line, ^R redraws the line (a line of 0x42 or more characters instead gets `^R`, a newline and the prompt `? ` before the line is printed again), ^V takes the next character literally through `showchar`, ^N and ^P walk the ten-line history (a bell character, 7, when there is nowhere to go), newline and carriage return finish the line, tab is turned into a space. A printable character is inserted at the cursor with `bcopy` of the tail and the rest of the line is redrawn with backspaces; when the line reaches 0xfd characters or the character is not printable a bell is sent instead.
  After a line completes, a line starting `!` recalls history: `!!` alone selects the previous line and the selected line is echoed; a non-empty line is stored into the next history slot, the current index advances modulo 10 and the new slot is cleared. It returns the address of the finished line; the loop has no other exit, since ^D on an empty line enters the PROM monitor instead of returning.
`EnterInteractiveMode`, `bcopy`, `exec_brkpt`, `getc`, `ioctl`, `printf`, `putchar`, `showchar`, `strlen`).
- `_rdebug` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the serial-line remote debugging command, a 1664-byte routine at address 0xa001d260. Inferred.
  The arguments are the command's argument count (a0) and vector (a1). With two operands and the first equal to `-r` it treats the call as a restart after the user suspended the session (skipping the greeting); it then requires exactly one operand, the console device name, and returns 1 otherwise.
  It opens the device read-write (`Open`, mode 2); on failure it prints `can't open %s (error %d)` and returns 0. On success it registers the descriptor with `close_on_exception`, switches the line to packet framing (`proto_enable`) and, unless this is a restart, runs `init_proto` and sends the first reply, a packet built from the string `0x1 b junk` through the reply routine at 0xa001dd14 with the stop status for signal 5 (SIGTRAP, status helper at 0xa001dbdc with 0x7f).
  Then it loops on `getpkt` (maximum 0x3ff bytes). A negative length prints `ABNORMAL PROTOCOL TERMINATION`, clears the two jump-buffer words of the per-CPU block (`gen_pda_tab`+76 and +144), turns framing off (`proto_disable`), closes the device and returns 0. A packet is split into fields (`_argvize`); fewer than three fields gets an error reply (status -1, code 7) and also ends the session the same way. Otherwise a recovery buffer is armed (a fault inside the request sends an error reply built by 0xa001d8e0, which maps a stopped state to a signal number for `c` and `s`) and the first character of the second field selects the request: `i`/`d` read a word, `I`/`D` write a word, `r` read a register, `R` write one (register number checked by 0xa001de70), `g` return all registers (0xa001dbf0), `c` continue, `s` single step, `x` terminate; any other letter gets the error reply. `I` reads the old word first and replies with it: when the old word is the break instruction and the new one is not it removes the breakpoint (`old_brkpt`), when the new word is the break instruction and the old one is not it installs one (`new_brkpt`), when both are it prints `overwriting bp with bp - ignored` and replies with an error, and otherwise it stores the word with `_set_memory`. `c` and `s` store an optional new program counter in the saved register block, then resume (`_resume_brkpt`) or step (`single_step`) and do not return to the loop. `x` replies with success, prints `Exiting debug monitor`, clears the buffers, closes the line and ends in the `jal` to `_quit` at 0xa001d468.
  Return values: 1 for a wrong operand count, 0 for open failure and for both ways the loop ends; `x` does not return.
- `printregs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the register display command, a 1664-byte routine at address 0xa0014608. Inferred.
  The arguments are the argument count (a0) and vector (a1). An option `-u` selects the user registers, `-s` the system registers and `-a` or `-b` both; the choice is stored in the word at 0xa00385a8 (bit 0 user, bit 1 system; 1 in the shipped image) and survives between calls. Any other option letter prints `Illegal printregs option (-)` and returns 1.
  With bit 0 set it prints `User-context registers:` and, through `_get_register` (0xa001b1c8), v0-v1, the eight argument registers a0-a7, four temporaries (t0-t3), s0-s7, t8, t9, at, k0, k1 and gp, sp, fp, ra, in the 64-bit hex formats of the N32 build.
  With bit 1 set it prints `System registers:` and EPC and status, cause and bad virtual address, the TLB entry high and low words, wired, page mask, index, random, context and extended context, cache error, error EPC (with the ECC word), the tag low words (formatted with the primary or secondary descriptor tables chosen by a processor-implementation test: PRId bits 15:8 below 0x23 picks the older descriptors), watch, count and compare, and config. For the older implementations it also prints the cache-error line naming the failing cache (`icache`, `dcache`, `scache` or `sysad` from the top two bits of the cache-error register).
  It returns 0, or 1 after an illegal option.
- `symmon_brkhandler` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the six exception-entry stubs and the common save code that follow it, a 1608-byte block at address 0xa0010350. Established.
  The block holds, in order, `symmon_brkhandler` (0xa0010350, reached by a client's breakpoint, entry code 3), `symmon_exceptnorm` (0xa0010370, code 1), `symmon_exceptutlb` (0xa001038c, code 2), `symmon_exceptxut` (0xa00103a8, code 4), `symmon_exceptecc` (0xa00103c4, code 5), `symmon_nmi` (0xa00103e0, code 6) and `exception` (0xa001041c). Each of the first five loads k1 with the per-CPU block at `gen_pda_tab` (0xa0058db8, one 512-byte block per CPU; these stubs use CPU 0's block directly), saves the client's `at` into the register block pointed at by block word +72, stores its code in block word +136 and goes on to `exception` with a `j`; the breakpoint stub first copies k0 into `at`. The NMI stub first clears the status register, then does the same save with code 6 and also writes 1 to block word +128 (the mode word) so the handler treats it as client mode.
  `exception` reads the mode word. Mode 1 (a client was running) switches to the debugger stack held at block word +120, saving the client sp there; any other mode pushes a new frame on the current stack (128 bytes). It then stores in that frame the saved k1 (block word +104), gp (and reloads gp with the debugger's 0xa0044908), EPC, status, bad virtual address, cause and ra, followed by eight CRIME registers read from KSEG1 0xb4000000: +0x40, +0x48, +0x58 (error address registers), +0x250, +0x258 (memory error status and address), +0x260, +0x268 and +0x270 (ECC syndrome, generated check bits and replacement check bits), stored at frame +64 to +120.
  It then writes zero to the status register (the code ands it with 0 and ors in 0), copies the mode word to block word +132 and clears it. A previous mode of zero (the debugger itself faulted) skips the register save. Otherwise it saves the client state into the register block (pointer at block word +72): every general register (zero is stored as 0, the k0 slot receives the marker 0x0bad0bad and the k1 slot the k1 saved in the frame), HI and LO, EPC, status, the entry code, bad virtual address, count, cause, index, random, context, entry-lo 0 and 1, entry-hi, page mask, wired, compare, lladdr, xcontext, ecc, cache error, tag low and high, error EPC and config, and watch low and high (stored as zero when the PRId implementation byte, bits 15:8 as a masked value, is 0x2000, 0x2100, 0x2300, 0x2700 or 0x2800; tested by subtracting 0x2000, 0x100, 0x200, 0x400 and 0x100 in turn).
  Unless the saved previous mode was 2, it calls `_ip32_disable_serial_dma` (0xa001140c), then, for implementation bytes other than 0x2300, 0x2700 and 0x2800, saves Config in `_ip32_config_reg` and, when its low three bits exceed 3, rewrites Config with those bits set to 3 and records the result in `_ip32_config_reg2`; `_save_vectors` (0xa001be54) is called on every path that did not take the early skip. The block ends by saving sp into block word +124 and then a `j` to `symmon_exchandler` (0xa0019f3c) with the frame as its argument; it never returns.
- `_tlbmap` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the command that edits one TLB entry by hand, a 1532-byte routine at address 0xa0015d94. Inferred.
  The arguments are the argument count (a0) and vector (a1). With no operand it prints the usage lines (`tlbmap [-i INDEX] -0 ...`, `-1`, `-H [VFN] [ASID]`) and returns 1.
  Option letters may be grouped after one `-`: `-0`, `-1` and `-H` pick which register to write (TLB entry low 0, low 1 or high; only one may be given); `-i` takes an index operand (default 8, rejected when negative or above 0x40 on a processor whose implementation byte, PRId bits 15:8, is below 0x23, and above 0x30 otherwise); `-c` takes a cache-attribute operand and fills bits 5:3 of the low word (refused with `-H`); `-v`, `-d` and `-g` set the valid (bit 1), dirty (bit 2) and global (bit 0) bits (refused with `-H`); any other letter prints `unknown flag` and returns 1. With no `-0`, `-1` or `-H` it prints `Must specify -0 (tlblo0), -1 (tlblo1) or -H (tlbhi)` and returns 1.
  It reads the current entry at the index with `get_tlblo0`, `get_tlblo1` and `get_tlbhi`. For `-0` or `-1` the optional operand is a page frame number (shifted left 6 to line up with the entry; bad number prints `bad pfn`), with no operand the existing frame bits (mask 0x3fffffc0) are kept; extra operands print `extraneous arguments`. For `-H` it takes an optional virtual page number and ASID (the current ones by default), and when `probe_tlb` finds the new address already mapped at a different index it prints that other entry and returns 0 without writing.
  Otherwise the global bit is applied to both low words and `tlbwired` writes the entry at the index; the entry is then read back and printed as `index: lo0 lo1 hi`. On success the instruction at 0xa0015f9c clears v0 before the shared epilogue at 0xa0015e44, so the result is 0; each error exit above sets v0 to 1 with an `li` first.
- `config_cache` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the cache sizes from the processor's Config register and fills the cache-geometry words, a 1392-byte routine at address 0xa0022ee8. Established.
  It takes no argument and returns nothing. It saves Status, reads Config and PRId, and uses the PRId word masked with 0xff00 (written here as "the implementation value") to pick the decoding. Part of it runs through the uncached alias of its own address (a `jr` through address OR 0xa0000000 at 0xa0023094 and at 0xa00231cc); Status is restored at 0xa002343c before the return.
  Sizes. For the implementation values 0x2300, 0x2700 and 0x2800 the instruction cache size is 1 shifted left by 12 plus Config bits 11:9, stored to `picache_size`, `_icache_size`, `icache_size` and `rpicache_size`, and the data cache size is 1 shifted left by 12 plus Config bits 8:6. For every other value the instruction size uses Config bits 31:29 and is stored only to `picache_size` and `rpicache_size`, and the data size uses bits 28:26. The data size goes to `pdcache_size`, `_dcache_size`, `dcache_size` and `rpdcache_size` in both cases.
  For the value 0x2700 with Config bit 31 clear it also acts on Config bit 3: when the bit is clear it zeroes the tag registers, sets the bit, and walks 0x40000 bytes from address 0x80000000 in 32-byte steps with the cache instruction (operation field 0xb); when the bit is set it skips that walk. Both paths then store 0x40000 to `picache_size`, `pdcache_size`, `ocache_enabled` and `_ocache_size`, and 32 to `_ocache_linesize`.
  Every path then stores 31 to `_icache_linemask`, `_dcache_linemask`, `scache_linemask`, `_scache_linemask` and `dmabuf_linemask`, and 32 to `_icache_linesize` and `_dcache_linesize`, reaches `size_2nd_cache` (0xa0023458, by a `jal` at 0xa00231d4 inside the uncached alias) and stores its result in `boot_sidcache_size` and `_sidcache_size`.
  A zero result (no secondary cache) copies `picache_size` to `icache_size` and `_icache_size` and `pdcache_size` to `dcache_size` and `_dcache_size`. Then, for the implementation values 0x2000, 0x2100, 0x2300 and 0x2800, it stores half of the data cache size in `two_set_pcaches`; for 0x2700 it stores a quarter of `rpdcache_size` in `four_set_pcaches` and half of it in `two_set_pcaches`. The word `cachecolorsize` is the stored `two_set_pcaches` value shifted right by 12 for those values, and the data cache size shifted right by 12 for any other value; `cachecolormask` is one less than `cachecolorsize`.
  A non-zero result makes the same two copies, then for the same values (0x2000, 0x2100, 0x2300, 0x2800 and 0x2700) it also stores 31 to `scache_linemask`, `_scache_linemask` and `dmabuf_linemask` and 32 to `_scache_linesize`, followed by the same `four_set_pcaches`, `two_set_pcaches`, `cachecolorsize` and `cachecolormask` stores as above. For any other implementation value it leaves those words alone and returns.
  All 348 words at 0xa0022ee8 to 0xa0023457 were read, including the two kseg1 jumps; the single subroutine is `size_2nd_cache`.
- `bcopy` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: byte-copy with overlap handling, a 1384-byte routine at address 0xa002ca40. Established.
  The arguments are source (a0), destination (a1) and byte count (a2); it returns nothing. A zero count or equal pointers returns at once. When the destination is above the source and below the source's end address (source plus count) it copies backward, otherwise forward.
  Forward, a count below 15 goes to a byte loop (`lb`/`sb`). Otherwise, if source and destination agree modulo 8, it copies the first unaligned bytes with one `ldl`/`sdl` pair, then 64 bytes per pass with eight `ld`/`sd` pairs, then optional 32-, 16- and 8-byte blocks selected by bits 5, 4 and 3 of the remaining count, and the final 1 to 7 bytes with one `ldr`/`sdr` pair. If they disagree modulo 8 the same structure runs with `ldl`/`ldr` pairs for each load and `sdl`/`sdr` pairs for each store, so no access is aligned, and the tail goes to the byte loop.
  Backward is the mirror image (pointers moved to the end, `ld`/`sd` blocks at negative offsets with `ldr`/`sdr` for the edge bytes, a byte loop descending with `addiu -1`). Every one of the 346 words from 0xa002ca40 to 0xa002cfa3 was read; it has no callee and no stack frame.
- `_bp_fault` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the breakpoint branch of the fault handler, a 1152-byte routine at address 0xa00129fc. Inferred.
  The argument is a flag (a0) saying the stop came from a watchpoint. It first waits out a one-instruction single step on another CPU: when the global `step1_cpu` equals this CPU it ORs the saved interrupt-enable bit into the client's status and sets `step1_cpu` to -1; when it is another CPU it spins until it is -1. It reads the `regstyle` environment string with `getenv`, converts it with `atob`, then takes the breakpoint address from the client's EPC (the cause register's branch-delay bit moves it by one word) and looks the address up with the table lookup at 0xa0012934, which returns a type: 3 (a temporary stepping breakpoint), 0 (none), 4 (permanent), 2 (continue) or 1 (suspended).
  Type 3: removes every temporary breakpoint and re-installs each suspended one (`old_brkpt`, `_set_memory` with the break word `__kernel_bp`), checks for a watchpoint trip (0xa00138c0), prints the instruction at the client's EPC with `_PrintInstruction`, then either steps again through the routine pointer held in the per-CPU block (word +164, count word +160) or, when a stepping flag is set, calls `symmon_spl` and `longjmp`s back to the command loop.
  Type 0: if the client's a0 points into an address range the code accepts and the text there starts with `ring` or `zing`, it advances EPC by 4, prints `Keyboard interrupt`, and clears the stepping flag; if it starts with `quiet` it advances EPC by 4 silently; a watchpoint stop runs the handler at 0xa0013808; anything else advances EPC by 4 and prints `Unexpected breakpoint` and the instruction.
  Type 4: calls 0xa00138c0, prints `Breakpoint` and the instruction, and when a stepping flag is set longjmps to the command loop. Type 2: removes the continue breakpoint, re-installs suspended ones and removes any other continue breakpoint, then calls `_resume_brkpt`, which does not return. Type 1 prints `cpu %d hit a SUSPENDed breakpoint`, and type 1 or any unknown type ends in `_fatal_error`.
  Every path that returns normally ends with `_restart` (0xa00184a0), whose value is the return.
