- `tlbwired` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes one TLB entry at an index from its arguments, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa0024884. Established. The body never reads or writes the Wired register, so it is an indexed write at whatever index the caller gives, not a write at the wired boundary.
  The five arguments are the index (first, `a0`), a value that is OR-ed into EntryHi (second, `a1`), a virtual address of which only bit 13 and above are kept (third, `a2`, ANDed with -8192 and OR-ed into the second), EntryLo0 (fourth, `a3`) and EntryLo1 (fifth, `a4`, held in a register as the N32 convention passes it). It saves EntryHi and Status, clears Status bit 0 (the `ori 1` then `xori 1` pair) so no interrupt arrives mid-write, loads EntryHi, EntryLo0, EntryLo1 and Index in that order, executes `tlbwi` after one `nop`, and puts EntryHi and Status back. PageMask is not written, so the entry takes whatever page size is already set. It returns nothing; `v0` still holds the saved Status word. The one direct call is at 0xa0015f40, inside `_tlbmap`.

- `tlbwired_sc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the same TLB write with the fifth argument read from the stack and the whole of Status cleared, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa00248d8. Established.
  The order and the registers are those of `tlbwired` (index in `a0`, a value OR-ed into EntryHi in `a1`, the address in `a2`, EntryLo0 in `a3`). The differences are: it writes zero to Status outright (`mtc0 zero`) instead of clearing one bit; the address mask is the constant 8191 shifted left 19, which keeps bits 31 to 19 of the third argument instead of 63 to 13; and the EntryLo1 value is the word loaded from `16(sp)` (an o32-style stack argument) at 0xa00248f0. It saves and restores EntryHi and Status, writes EntryHi, EntryLo0, EntryLo1 and Index, and issues `tlbwi` after one `nop`. It returns nothing and I found no direct caller.

- `invaltlb` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: overwrites one TLB entry with an unusable value, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa0024838. Established; that the value cannot match a mapped address is inferred from 0x80000000 lying in the unmapped kernel segment.
  The argument is the index. It saves Status, clears bit 0 of Status, saves EntryHi, writes EntryHi with 0x80000000 (`lui` of 0x8000), EntryLo0 and EntryLo1 with zero and Index with the argument, issues `tlbwi` after one `nop`, then restores EntryHi and Status. It returns nothing (`v0` is the saved Status). It is called at 0xa00102fc in `start` and 0xa001599c in `_tlbflush`.

- `get_pgmaski` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the page-mask register of the TLB entry at an index, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa0024700. Established.
  It saves Status in `t0`, clears Status bit 0, saves EntryHi in `t1`, writes the argument to Index, issues `tlbr`, waits three `nop`s, reads PageMask into `v0` and restores EntryHi and Status. It returns that PageMask value. Four direct calls exist, at 0xa0015768, 0xa0015794, 0xa0015a98 and 0xa0015ac8.

- `get_tlbhi` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the EntryHi of the TLB entry at an index, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0024768. Established.
  It is the `get_pgmaski` sequence (Status saved and bit 0 cleared, EntryHi saved, Index written, `tlbr`, three `nop`s, original EntryHi and Status restored) with the value read after `tlbr` being EntryHi itself, which it returns in `v0`. It has seven direct callers, at 0xa0015750, 0xa0015788, 0xa0015abc, 0xa0015c58, 0xa0015eec, 0xa0015f60 and 0xa0016318.

- `get_tlblo0` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the first EntryLo word of one TLB entry, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0024670 (6.5.30, N32). Established.
  The argument in a0 is the TLB index. It saves Status in t0 and writes it back with bit 0 cleared (`ori` then `xori` with 1, at 0xa0024678 and 0xa002467c), saves EntryHi in t1, writes the index to the Index register and executes `tlbr` at 0xa0024694. Three `nop` words follow it (the listing elides them), then `mfc0 v0,c0_entrylo0` at 0xa00246a4 puts EntryLo0 in the return value. It restores EntryHi and Status from t1 and t0 and returns through `jr ra` at 0xa00246b0 with a `nop` in the delay slot. There is one exit and no callee. The disassembly labels the address `get_tlblo0`, and the next routine, `get_tlblo1` (0xa00246b8), has the same shape. Eight `jal` sites call it (0xa00157a0, 0xa0015ad4, 0xa0015bf0, 0xa0015c40, 0xa0015ed4, 0xa0015f48, 0xa0016300 and 0xa001b344).
- `get_tlblo` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the second symbol of the 72-byte body at address 0xa0024670 (6.5.30, N32), which returns EntryLo0 of the TLB entry selected by its argument. Established.
  The symbol table gives `get_tlblo` and `get_tlblo0` the same address and size, so the body is the one described under `get_tlblo0`: it disables interrupts through Status bit 0, writes the argument to Index, runs `tlbr` and reads EntryLo0 at 0xa00246a4, restoring EntryHi and Status before the return at 0xa00246b0. Every `jal` in the image names the `get_tlblo0` label, so no call site is specific to this symbol.

- `get_tlblo1` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads EntryLo1 of the TLB entry at an index, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa00246b8. Established.
  It is `get_tlblo` with EntryLo1 (CP0 register 3) read after `tlbr` instead of EntryLo0. Direct callers are at 0xa00157ac, 0xa0015ae0, 0xa0015c14, 0xa0015c4c, 0xa0015ee0, 0xa0015f54, 0xa001630c and 0xa001b370.

- `_is_kp_func` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: asks whether a name is in the debugger's function table, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa001a6c0. Established for the code; the role is inferred from the callee and the routine's name.
  It saves its argument in `s0` and calls the routine at 0xa001a0e4 (no arguments). That routine reads the pointer at 0xa0001010. A null pointer, or a word at offset 8 of the pointed-to record that lies outside 0x80000000 up to 0xc0000000, makes it store zero at 0xa003cd68 and return 0. Otherwise it treats that word as an address, stores it at 0xa003cd68 (the store is in the delay slot of the compare, so it happens on a mismatch too) and compares the word found there with 0x45454545; on a match it also stores the doubleword loaded from address plus 4 at 0xa003cd58 and returns 1, and on a mismatch it returns 0. When that call returned 0, or the argument is 0, `_is_kp_func` returns 0. Otherwise it calls the routine at 0xa001a71c with the argument, the word at 0xa003cd68 and the address `sp+0` of a local word, and returns that call's result. From the first third of that routine, which I read and did not follow to its end, it prints "Available Functions:" when its first argument is 0, and otherwise walks 20-byte records ending at a word 0x54545454, comparing names with `strncmp` (0xa0030d60). No direct caller exists.

- `check_alarm` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: fires a one-shot software alarm when its deadline has passed, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa0024dfc. Established.
  The two words involved are unnamed `.bss` at 0xa0058ce4 (the deadline) and 0xa0058ce8 (the armed flag). With the flag zero it returns at once. Otherwise it calls `GetRelativeTime` (0xa002adc8) and compares unsigned: while the time is below the deadline it returns with `v0` holding the time. When the time has reached the deadline it clears the flag (the store sits in the delay slot of the call) and calls `psignal` (0xa00249e0) with signal number 14, returning that result, which is 0 because 14 is in range. The one direct call is at 0xa0026da0.

- `dLine` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: copies one primary data-cache line and its tag into a buffer, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa0023e54. Established; that `cache` operation 0x19 reads the data is inferred, as for operation 0x18 in `iLine`.
  The first argument is the line address and the second the buffer. It issues `cache` 0x5 (index load tag, primary data cache), shifts TagHi left 32, ORs TagLo into it and stores that doubleword at buffer offset 0. It then makes eight passes with the address stepping by 4 (the loop ends when the address reaches the start plus 32, tested with a signed compare after the first pass): each issues `cache` 0x19 on the address, stores TagLo as a 32-bit word from buffer offset 8 in steps of 4, and stores the CP0 ECC register as a byte from buffer offset 40 in steps of 1. It returns nothing.

- `dumpSecondaryLine` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints one secondary-cache line for a cache index, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa0017fb0. Established.
  The arguments are an index (`a0`), a value added to the address (`a1`, which `dumpSecondaryCache` passes as 0 then 1) and a flag (`a2`). It forms the address as the index shifted left 6 plus 0x80000000, zero-extended to 32 bits, and keeps it at `104(sp)`. It calls `sLine` (0xa0023ea8) with that address plus `a1` and a local buffer at `sp+0`, then calls the routine at 0xa0017e9c with the buffer, the saved address and the flag, and returns that call's result. The routine at 0xa0017e9c starts by asking for the secondary cache size, prints the decoded tag fields with `printf` (0xa002e620) and, when its flag is non-zero, prints four more lines of two doublewords and one halfword each.

- `dumpSecondaryLineAddr` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the address-driven form of the secondary-cache line dump, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa0018004. Established.
  The body differs from `dumpSecondaryLine` only in how it forms the address: the first argument is ORed with 0x80000000 (a zero-extended constant built from 0x8000 shifted left 16) and ANDed with -64, which clears the low six bits and keeps the upper bits, and the result is saved at `104(sp)`. It then calls `sLine` (0xa0023ea8) with the second argument plus that address and a local buffer at `sp+0`, and the routine at 0xa0017e9c with the buffer, the saved address and the third argument, returning that result.

- `getc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads one byte from a descriptor, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa002d368. Established.
  It calls `Read` (0xa00251ec) with its own first argument unchanged, a one-byte buffer at `sp+0`, a length of 1 and the address `sp+4` for the count. It returns the byte (loaded with `lbu`, so 0 to 255) only when `Read` returns 0 and the count word equals 1. A non-zero return from `Read` or any other count calls `_errgetc` (0xa002a890), which is `li v0,-1; jr ra`, so the routine returns -1.

- `idbg_cleanup` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: removes the debugger's hook records when the debugged program ends, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa001a174. Established for the code; the purpose is inferred.
  It reads the pointer at 0xa0001010 into `s0` (the load sits in the delay slot of the first call), calls `validate_brkpts` (0xa00127f4) with no argument set, then calls the routine at 0xa001a0e4 (described under `_is_kp_func`). If the word at 0xa003cd68 is non-zero it stores zero at offset 0 of the address that word holds (the first word of the table that routine validated). Then, only when `s0` is non-zero (a branch-likely at 0xa001a1a8 whose delay slot is annulled when `s0` is non-zero), it stores zero at offsets 12, 16 and 8 of the record `s0` points to, in that order. It returns the result of the 0xa001a0e4 routine.

- `initFonts` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: fills in the on-screen text console's font record and links it into the console state, an 84-byte routine at address 0xa001fd4c. Established for the stores; reading the record as a font description comes from the data names `ScrB18Data` and `ScrB18Info`.
  It takes no arguments. It loads the word at `htp` (0xa003aa98) and stores the address 0xa0056998 at offset 8 of what that word points to. It then fills the 0xa0056998 record, in this order: halfword 4 at offset 22, halfword 20 at 20, the address 0xa003aa60 at 16, the address 0xa003aa08 at 12, halfword 4 at 10, halfword 17 at 8, the address 0xa003a418 (`ScrB18Info`) at 4, and last, in the delay slot of the return, the address 0xa0039378 (`ScrB18Data`, 0x109c bytes) at offset 0. `v0` is left as 0xa0050000. It is called from `htp_init`.

- `_digit` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: converts a character to its digit value for number parsing, an 80-byte routine at address 0xa002bdc8. Established.
  The argument is the character code; the class byte is `__ctype` (0xa003c528) at index code plus 1, tested through `lbu`. A class bit 4 (decimal digit) returns the code minus 48. Otherwise, when neither of the two low class bits is set, a branch-likely at 0xa002bdf0 takes its delay slot and the routine returns 0xf423f (999999, formed by `lui` 0xf and `ori` 0x423f). With bit 0 set (upper-case) the code is first replaced by the byte at `__ctype` plus 258 plus the code, a case-conversion table in which 'A' maps to 'a'; with only bit 1 set the code is used as is. Either way the result is that code minus 87, so 'a' gives 10. There is no check that the letter is within 'f': 'g' returns 16. The 0x4000 part of the `andi` mask 0x4003 cannot be set in a byte.

- `_get_register` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: returns one saved register of the stopped program by number, an 80-byte routine at address 0xa001b1c8. Established.
  The argument is a register number. The saved registers are doublewords in an array whose address is the word at 0xa0058e00; the routine returns the doubleword at that address plus the number times 8. A number below 93 (a signed `slti`) goes straight to that load. A number of 93 or more first calls `_dbg_error` (0xa0018428) with the string "invalid register number" (0xa0035308) and a second argument of 0, and then falls into the same load, so a call to `_dbg_error` that returns would still read the out-of-range slot; negative numbers also pass the test. It is called six times from `printregs` (0xa0014608), the first at 0xa001466c.

- `delete_strnum` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: removes entry number n from a table of words and closes the gap, an 80-byte routine at address 0xa002b8ec. Established.
  The first argument is the entry number and the second the table record, whose word at offset 1540 is the entry count and whose entries are words from offset 0. It returns 1 when the number is negative or at least the count. Otherwise it copies the word that follows entry n over entry n, and keeps copying each next word down by one slot until the word it just moved is zero, so the zero that ends the list moves down too; it returns 0. The count at offset 1540 is only read, never written. I found no direct caller.

- `strncmp` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: compares two strings for at most a given number of bytes, an 80-byte routine at address 0xa0030d60. Established.
  The first two arguments are the strings and the third the limit. A limit of 0 returns 0. Each pass loads one unsigned byte from each string and advances both pointers; unequal bytes return the first string's byte minus the second's. Equal bytes return 0 when the byte is zero (the end of both strings) and otherwise count the limit down and stop with 0 once it is exhausted, so at most the limit's worth of bytes is read from each string. It is called at 0xa001a804 and 0xa001a818, both inside the routine at 0xa001a71c that searches 20-byte name records.

- `CRCengine` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: advances an 8-bit Dallas-style CRC by one input bit, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa002782c. Established for the arithmetic; naming it the Dallas 1-wire CRC (polynomial x^8 + x^5 + x^4 + 1, whose reflected form flips bits 7, 3 and 2 on feedback) is inferred from matching the step by hand.
  The first argument is the input bit (only its bit 0 is used) and the second the address of the byte that holds the CRC, loaded with `lbu` and stored back with `sb` at 0xa0027874 (in the delay slot of the return). With c the byte, the feedback bit is bit 0 of c XOR the input. The new byte is c shifted right one with the result masked by 0x73 (bits 0, 1, 4, 5 and 6 of the new byte), bit 7 set to the feedback, and bits 2 and 3 set to the feedback XOR bits 3 and 4 of c respectively. It returns nothing. It is called six times from the DS2502 read routines, at 0xa0028a9c, 0xa0028ad8 and 0xa0028b14 in `ds2502_read_rom`, and at 0xa0028bcc, 0xa0028cc0 and 0xa0028cf8 in `ds2502_read_ram`, once per bit, with `srav` and `andi 1` forming the bit.

- `_get_numcpus` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: counts the processors, from the configuration tree when one exists and from the firmware vector otherwise, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa00316b0. Established.
  It calls `GetChild` (0xa00241c8) with 0, which returns the first child of the tree root (the word at 0xa0058c8c). With a null child it returns what `kl_get_num_cpus` (0xa0030f40) returns. With a child it calls `GetChild` with 0 again and passes the result to the routine at 0xa003163c, returning that count. The routine at 0xa003163c sums, over a node and recursively over its children and peers (`GetChild` and `GetPeer`, 0xa00241c0), a 1 for each node whose first word is 1 and whose word at offset 4 is 1. It is called once, from `_init_libsc_private` (0xa00316fc).

- `close_closeonexec` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: closes every low descriptor flagged close-on-exec, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa002b104. Established.
  It takes no arguments and walks the descriptors 0 to 19. For each it calls `iscloseonexec` (0xa002ca18); a non-zero answer calls `Close` (0xa00255a0) on the descriptor. The test is a branch-likely at 0xa002b124 (its delay slot, the counter increment, runs only when it is taken); the not-taken path increments after `Close`. The loop ends when the counter reaches 20. It returns nothing. I found no direct caller.

- `console_is_gfx` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: says whether the console is a graphics display, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa0026f24. Established.
  It takes no arguments. If the word at 0xa005a3e4 is non-zero, or else the word at 0xa0058d1c is non-zero, it calls `isgraphic` (0xa002b028) with 1 and returns that result. When both words are zero it returns 0 without calling. It is called from `_errputc` (0xa002a854).

- `ffs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: returns the position of the lowest set bit of a word, counting from 1, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa0024a90. Established.
  A zero argument returns 0. An argument with bit 0 set returns 1. Otherwise it starts at 0 and, in a loop closed by a branch-likely at 0xa0024ab4 (its delay slot, the increment, runs only when the branch is taken), increments the counter, builds 1 shifted left by it with `sllv` and tests that mask against the argument; it returns the counter plus 1 when the test is non-zero. The counter has no bound. `issig` (0xa0024a1c) calls it at 0xa0024a28 on the pending-signal word.

- `fs_init` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: runs the start-up routines of the built-in file systems, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa002633c. Established.
  It stores zero at 0xa0058d14, then reads the table `_fs_table` (0xa003936c), a zero-terminated list of function pointers, and calls each entry in order through `jalr` with no arguments set by it, ignoring the results. In this image the first word of the table is 0, so no call is made. It returns nothing. It is called once, from `_init_saio` (0xa002664c).

- `kern_calloc` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: allocates zeroed memory for a count of items of a given size, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa002fe70. Established.
  The two arguments are multiplied with `multu` and the low 32 bits taken as the size; the high word is not tested, so an overflowing product wraps. It calls `malloc` (0xa002fb10) with that size. A null result is returned as it is; otherwise `bzero` (0xa002cfa8) clears the block, with the size reloaded from `0(sp)`, and the routine returns the block's address.

- `strdup` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: makes a malloc'd copy of a string, a 76-byte routine at address 0xa00309d8. Established.
  It calls `strlen` (0xa00309ac) on its argument, kept at `0(sp)`, then `malloc` (0xa002fb10) with the length plus 1. A null result returns 0. Otherwise `strcpy` (0xa0030978) copies the original into the new block and the routine returns the block's address (kept in `s0`).

- `longjmp` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: restores the registers saved by `setjmp` and returns to the saved return address, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0030318. Established.
  The first argument is the buffer, thirteen doublewords. It loads, in this order, `ra` from offset 0, `s8` from 16, `sp` from 8, `s0` to `s7` from 24 through 80 in steps of 8, `s8` again from 88 and, in the delay slot of the branch, `t9` from 96. The second argument becomes the return value, except that a zero second argument returns 1 (the `li` at 0xa0030354 runs only when the branch is not taken). `setjmp` (0xa00302dc) fills the same slots, storing `s8` at both 16 and 88, and returns 0. It is called from eleven sites: 0xa0011158, 0xa0012c30, 0xa0012d74, 0xa00133fc, 0xa0018490 (in `_dbg_error`), 0xa00184cc, 0xa001a02c, 0xa001be0c, 0xa001cd94, 0xa001cddc and 0xa0030c60.

- `_show_inst` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: prints the instruction at an address in the register style the environment names, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0018608. Established.
  It keeps its argument in `s8`, calls `getenv` (0xa002ba5c) with the string "regstyle" (0xa0033708), converts the result with `atob` (0xa002bea0) into a word at `sp+0` and calls `_PrintInstruction` (0xa0018650) with the original argument, that word and a third argument of 1, returning its result. The `atob` return value is not tested, and the word at `sp+0` is read whether or not `atob` stored it. Its one direct call is at 0xa0011270 in `symmon_fault`.

- `free_eiob` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: releases an open-file record and clears its slot in the descriptor table, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0026e78. Established.
  The argument is the record. If its word at offset 92 is non-zero that pointer is passed to `free` (0xa002fd18). It then reads the word at offset 96 (the descriptor number), stores zero at 0xa0058d28 plus 4 times that number (the table `get_eiob` reads), the store sitting in the delay slot of the second call, and calls `free` on the record itself. It returns what that last `free` returns. Callers are 0xa0024ec0 in `Open`, 0xa002561c in `Close`, 0xa002596c and 0xa00259b4 in `Mount`, and 0xa00267f8 in `_init_saio`.

- `psig` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: delivers one signal by calling its handler, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0024a48. Established.
  The argument is the signal number n, used with no range test. It clears bit n minus 1 of the pending-signal word at 0xa0058ce0 (the store is in the delay slot of the call) and calls the handler pointer at 0xa0058c90 plus 4 times n through `jalr`. No argument is set up for the handler: `a0` still holds the complemented bit mask `nor a0,a0,zero` left. It returns the handler's return value. `psignal` (0xa00249e0) sets those pending bits and `Signal` (0xa002495c) writes the handler table. The one direct call is at 0xa0026d7c in `_scandevs`.

- `rbsetbs` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: sets flag bits in the firmware restart block and refreshes its checksum, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa0031294. Established for the code; calling the record a restart block follows its address, the word at 0xa000100c in the system parameter block.
  The argument is the set of bits. It loads the pointer at 0xa000100c and returns doing nothing when it is null. Otherwise it ORs the argument into the word at offset 28 of the record, stores it back (in the delay slot of the call, so before the call runs), calls `checksum_rb` (0xa0031310) with the record and stores the result at offset 32. `checksum_rb` here is an 8-byte routine that returns 0, so offset 32 is set to 0. I found no direct caller.

- `strcat` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: appends one string to another, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa00308f0. Established.
  The first argument is the destination and the second the source; it returns the destination unchanged. It scans the destination for its zero byte, then copies the source byte by byte, storing each byte (including the final zero) at the position just past the previous one, and stops after storing the source's zero. It does not check the destination's size. There are fourteen direct calls, including 0xa001b5d8, 0xa0025cc8, 0xa002b7b0, 0xa002c390, 0xa00300bc and five in `fixupname`.

- `swapl` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reverses the bytes of each word in an array in place, a 72-byte routine at address 0xa002d258. Established.
  The first argument is the array and the second the word count; a count of 0 does nothing. Each pass loads a 32-bit word, builds the byte-reversed word from the shifts `sll 24`, `srl 24`, `andi 0xff00` shifted left 8 and the word shifted right 8 masked by 0xff00, stores it back and advances 4 bytes, decrementing the count until it reaches 0. It returns nothing (`v0` holds the last reversed word). I found no direct caller.

- `getpath` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: builds the textual path of a configuration-tree node in a static buffer, a 68-byte routine at address 0xa0030118. Established for the code; calling the text a path in the `name(number)` form comes from the callee's format string.
  It stores a zero byte at 0xa005a408 and calls the routine at 0xa0030074 with its own argument and that buffer. That routine, for a non-null node, recurses on the parent (`GetParent`, 0xa00241e4), appends the node's name from the routine at 0xa0030004 (for nodes whose word at offset 0 is 4, 5 or 6, the first word of the 12-byte record in the table at 0xa003c778 whose third word equals the node's word at offset 4, or "unknown" (0xa0037bc8) when none matches; an empty string for other classes), and when the name is non-empty adds the word at node offset 16 with the format "(%u)" (0xa0037bd0). `getpath` then returns 0 if the buffer's first byte is zero and the buffer address 0xa005a408 otherwise. Callers are 0xa0027050 in `_init_bootenv`, 0xa00303e8 in `fixupname` and 0xa00305a0 in `inv_findtape`.

- `initenv` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: copies the incoming environment strings into the debugger's own table, a 68-byte routine at address 0xa002bb94. Established.
  The argument is the address of a word holding the incoming string-pointer array. It calls `_copystrings` (0xa002b9b8) with that array and the address 0xa00597d0. Inside `_copystrings`, `init_str` (0xa002b39c) first zeroes the table's first word and its count at offset 1540 and sets the word at offset 1536 to the table address plus 256, then `new_str1` (0xa002b5dc) is applied to each non-zero pointer in the array, and the table address is the return value. `initenv` stores that result in `environ` (0xa00597c8) and back through its argument, and returns it. It is called from `start` (0xa0010304).

- `kl_get_num_cpus` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: asks the firmware's private vector for the processor count, a 68-byte routine at address 0xa0030f40. Established for the code; the table is the one at the pointer 0xa0001028, whose length word 0xa0001024 and layout match the ARCS system parameter block, an inference from those offsets.
  It reads the length word at 0xa0001024; when it is below 61 it returns 0. Otherwise it loads the pointer at 0xa0001028, loads the word at offset 60 of that table and calls it with `jalr`, with the caller's arguments untouched, returning the result. Its one direct caller is `_get_numcpus` at 0xa00316c8.

- `kl_hinv` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: forwards to the firmware's inventory routine, a 68-byte routine at address 0xa0030efc. Established.
  It has the shape of `kl_get_num_cpus` with the length limit 57 (it returns 0 below it) and the table word at offset 56. The caller's arguments reach the callee untouched and the callee's result is returned. I found no direct caller.

- `kl_inv_find` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: forwards to the firmware's inventory search, a 68-byte routine at address 0xa0030eb8. Established.
  It has the same shape with the length limit 53 (it returns 0 below it) and the table word at offset 52, called through `jalr` with the caller's arguments untouched. Its one direct call is at 0xa0030378, in `inv_findcpu`.
- `__dcache_inval` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: discards the data-cache lines covering an address range, an 892-byte routine at address 0xa0021e4c. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0) and byte count (a1); nothing is returned. A count of zero or less returns at once by branching to the `jr ra` of `__icache_inval` (0xa0022794). Operation codes below are the first operand of the `cache` instruction, in hex; the geometry words are the ones `config_cache` fills.
  Secondary cache, taken when `_sidcache_size` is non-zero and `two_set_pcaches` is zero: a range no larger than `_sidcache_size` is walked in 64-byte lines, a start or end that is not 64-byte aligned getting operation 0x17 on its partial line, every whole line operation 0x13, and one operation 0x14 on the last line before the return; a larger range runs operation 0x3 at offsets 0 and 1 over every 64-byte line of the first half of `_sidcache_size` from 0x80000000, then operation 0x14, and returns.
  Primary data cache, 32-byte lines (a zero `pdcache_size` branches to `_r4600sc_index_inval`, 0xa0021b6c). A range within `pdcache_size` gets operation 0x15 on partial lines and 0x11 on whole lines (0x17 and 0x13 after a `sync` when `four_set_pcaches` is non-zero); the routine then returns when both `two_set_pcaches` and `four_set_pcaches` are zero and otherwise ends at `_r4600sc_index_inval`. A larger range runs index operation 0x1 over every line of `pdcache_size` from 0x80000000 and returns when `two_set_pcaches` is zero; with it non-zero each step also hits the second way (address xor `two_set_pcaches`) and the routine ends at `_r4600sc_inval_cache` (0xa0021a0c); under `four_set_pcaches` the sweep is preceded by a `sync`, uses four way addresses (xor `rpdcache_size` >> 2, >> 1 and their sum) over a quarter of `rpdcache_size`, repeats with operation 0x3 across a quarter of `pdcache_size` when `ocache_enabled` is non-zero, and ends at `_r4600sc_inval_cache`.
  The whole 892 bytes were read; the two kseg1 aliasing branches into `_r4600sc_index_inval` and `_r4600sc_inval_cache` are the only exits that do not return directly.
- `__dcache_wb` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes the data-cache lines of an address range back to memory without invalidating them, a 684-byte routine at address 0xa00221c8. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0) and byte count (a1); a count of zero or less returns through the `jr ra` of `__icache_inval` (0xa0022794). It has the same decision tree as `__dcache_inval` (secondary cache when `_sidcache_size` is non-zero and `two_set_pcaches` is zero; otherwise the primary data cache with 32-byte lines, split on `four_set_pcaches`) but every path ends in `jr ra`; it never calls the R4600 secondary-cache routines. Operation codes are the first operand of the `cache` instruction, in hex.
  Secondary cache: a range no larger than `_sidcache_size` runs operation 0x17 at offsets 0 and 1 of each 64-byte line from the start address; a larger range runs operation 0x3 at offsets 0 and 1 over every 64-byte line of the first half of `_sidcache_size` from 0x80000000; both end with one operation 0x14 on the last line.
  Primary data cache, with `pdcache_size` zero returning at once: a range within `pdcache_size` gets operation 0x19 per 32-byte line (0x1b after a `sync` when `four_set_pcaches` is non-zero); a larger range runs operation 0x1 over every line of `pdcache_size` from 0x80000000, adding the second way (address xor `two_set_pcaches`) when that word is non-zero, or, under `four_set_pcaches`, a `sync` and four way addresses (xor `rpdcache_size` >> 2, >> 1 and their sum) across a quarter of `rpdcache_size`, followed when `ocache_enabled` is non-zero by the same four-way sweep with operation 0x3 across a quarter of `pdcache_size`.
  The whole 684 bytes were read; every exit is a plain `jr ra`, so none of the R4600 tag routines runs for a write-back.
- `__dcache_wb_inval` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes back and discards the data-cache lines of an address range, a 688-byte routine at address 0xa0022474. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0) and byte count (a1); a count of zero or less returns through the `jr ra` at 0xa0022794. It follows the same tree as `__dcache_wb` with hit operation 0x15 (0x17 after a `sync` under `four_set_pcaches`) on the primary data cache and operation 0x17 at offset 0 only on the secondary cache (a range within `_sidcache_size`, 64-byte lines, ended by operation 0x14; a larger range runs operation 0x3 at offsets 0 and 1 over half of `_sidcache_size`, then 0x14); both secondary paths return.
  Primary data cache, 32-byte lines: with `two_set_pcaches` zero and no four-set cache, a range within `pdcache_size` gets operation 0x15 per line and the routine returns, and a larger range runs index operation 0x1 over all of `pdcache_size` and returns. With `two_set_pcaches` non-zero, a range within the size gets operation 0x15 per line (skipped when the start address is zero) and the routine ends at `_r4600sc_index_inval` (0xa0021b6c), and a larger range runs operation 0x1 on each line and its second way (address xor `two_set_pcaches`) and ends at `_r4600sc_inval_cache` (0xa0021a0c). With `four_set_pcaches` non-zero, a range within `pdcache_size` gets a `sync` and operation 0x17 per line and ends at `_r4600sc_index_inval`; a larger range runs the four-way operation 0x1 sweep across a quarter of `rpdcache_size`, adds the operation 0x3 sweep when `ocache_enabled` is non-zero, and ends at `_r4600sc_inval_cache`. A zero `pdcache_size` branches to `_r4600sc_index_inval` directly. The whole 688 bytes were read; this is the only one of the three data-cache routines whose two-way hit loop tests for a zero start address.
- `__icache_inval` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: makes the instruction stream of an address range coherent, a 1148-byte routine at address 0xa0022724. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0) and byte count (a1); a count of zero or less returns at once (`jr ra`, 0xa0022794). Operation codes are the first operand of the `cache` instruction, in hex.
  Secondary cache, taken when `_sidcache_size` is non-zero and `two_set_pcaches` is zero: a range within `_sidcache_size` runs operation 0x17 per 64-byte line from the line-aligned start, a larger one runs operation 0x3 at offsets 0 and 1 over half of `_sidcache_size` from 0x80000000, and both finish with operation 0x14 and return.
  Otherwise it handles the data cache first and the instruction cache second, in 32-byte lines (64-byte lines for the instruction cache on the single-way path). Data cache, skipped when `pdcache_size` is zero: a range within the size gets operation 0x15 per line; a larger range runs operation 0x1 over all of `pdcache_size` from 0x80000000 (single way), over `two_set_pcaches` bytes with the second way at the address xor `two_set_pcaches`, or, under `four_set_pcaches`, over a quarter of `rpdcache_size` with four way addresses after a `sync`. Instruction cache, skipped when `picache_size` is zero: a range within the size gets operation 0x10 per line; a larger range runs operation 0x0 over the cache the same way (single way over `picache_size`, two ways over `two_set_pcaches` bytes, or four ways over a quarter of `rpicache_size`).
  Exits: the single-way path (no `two_set_pcaches`, no `four_set_pcaches`) returns with `jr ra`; the two-way path ends at `_r4600sc_index_inval` (0xa0021b6c) after the small hit loop and at `_r4600sc_inval_cache` (0xa0021a0c) after the large index loop; the four-way path, after both primary passes, runs a secondary pass when `ocache_enabled` is non-zero (operation 0x17 per line after a `sync` for a range within `pdcache_size`, otherwise four-way operation 0x3) and ends at `_r4600sc_inval_cache`. The whole 1148 bytes were read, including the shared `jr ra` at 0xa0022794 that the three data-cache routines branch to for an empty range.
- `__cache_wb_inval` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes back and discards the cache lines of an address range by index operations, choosing the sweep from the processor's cache description, an 840-byte routine at address 0xa0022ba0. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0) and byte count (a1). A count of zero or less returns at once through the `jr ra` at 0xa0022e40.
  It returns no defined value (`v0` holds whichever cache-size word was loaded last). Every `cache` operand below is given as the whole first operand of the instruction, in hex. The words it tests are `two_set_pcaches` (0xa005a670),
  `_sidcache_size` (0xa005a660), `pdcache_size` (0xa005a730), `picache_size` (0xa005a780),
  `four_set_pcaches` (0xa005a740), `rpdcache_size` (0xa005a7c0),
  `rpicache_size` (0xa005a6b0) and `ocache_enabled` (0xa005a6f0).
  When `two_set_pcaches` is zero and `_sidcache_size` is non-zero it takes the secondary-cache path; when `two_set_pcaches` is non-zero the secondary size is not tested and the primary paths run.
  Secondary-cache path (0xa0022e48): the span is the count capped at half of `_sidcache_size`; the start is rounded down to a 64-byte boundary and the last line address is the end less 64. When the count is below half of `_sidcache_size` it first sweeps the primary caches with a limit that is the smaller of the count and the start address plus half of `pdcache_size` (the count is compared with an address sum, as the instructions read), and per 64 bytes it issues operation 0x1 at offsets -32, -31, -64 and -63 from the advanced pointer and operation 0x0 at -64 and -63 (the last one in the loop branch's delay slot). A count of at least half of `_sidcache_size` skips that sweep.
  Then, at 64-byte steps, it issues operation 0x3 at offsets 0 and 1 of each line (a do-while, so at least one line), and returns after one operation 0x14 at -64 from the final pointer (0xa0022edc), the cache-barrier operation that the source's secondary-cache path issues after its loop.
  Primary paths with `two_set_pcaches` non-zero and `four_set_pcaches` zero: the data cache is skipped when `pdcache_size` is zero; otherwise the span is the count, or `two_set_pcaches` bytes when that is smaller than the count, and the start is rounded down to 32 bytes, the last line is the end less 32, and operation 0x1 runs at each line and at the line address xor `two_set_pcaches`.
  The instruction cache is skipped when `picache_size` is zero; otherwise the same span rule gives the limit, both ends are rounded to 32 bytes, and operation 0x0 runs at each line and its xor partner. Both paths end by a plain `b` to `_r4600sc_index_inval` (0xa0021b6c;
  sites 0xa0022c88 and 0xa0022dd8, nop in the delay slot), which also runs when a cache is skipped.
  With `four_set_pcaches` non-zero:
  the data sweep (no test of `pdcache_size`) takes a span of a quarter of `rpdcache_size` when that is below the count, else the count, rounds to 32 bytes, issues one `sync`, and per line runs operation 0x1 at the line and at its xor with a quarter, with a half, and with both of `rpdcache_size`.
  The instruction sweep is skipped when `picache_size` is zero, otherwise it does the same with operation 0x0 and `rpicache_size`, after one `sync`.
  When `ocache_enabled` is non-zero a third sweep follows with operation 0x3 and the same four-way pattern built from `pdcache_size` (its quarter and half), then the routine branches to `_r4600sc_index_inval`.
  Neither cache size set (`two_set_pcaches` and `_sidcache_size` both zero):
  the data cache gets operation 0x1 per 32-byte line over the whole count (skipped when `pdcache_size` is zero), and the instruction cache operation 0x0 per 64-byte line from a 64-byte-aligned start to the aligned last line (skipped when `picache_size` is zero), then it returns. Ten `jal` sites reach it: 0xa001b318,
  0xa001b384, 0xa00239e8, 0xa0026f98,
  0xa00298c4, 0xa00298f0, 0xa0029a1c,
  0xa0029a48, 0xa0029b60 and 0xa0029b88; no table word holds its address.
- `clear_cache` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the second symbol of the routine at 0xa0022ba0, the 840-byte write-back and invalidate of a cache range described in the `__cache_wb_inval` entry. Established.
  The symbol table gives both names the same address and size, so the code is the one read in full for that entry: start address in a0, byte count in a1, index operations on the primary caches and, on the boards that report a secondary-cache size, on the secondary cache. The ten call sites listed under `__cache_wb_inval` all reach 0xa0022ba0.
- `_r4600sc_index_inval` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: clears the secondary-cache tags covering an address range on the secondary-cache boards, a 736-byte routine at address 0xa0021b6c. Established.
  The arguments are the start address (a0) and byte count (a1), passed on by the primary-cache routines. It returns (`jr ra`, 0xa0021e44) without work when `_sidcache_size` is zero, when the data word at 0xa003beb0 is non-zero (it is 1 in the shipped image), or when the PRId implementation value, masked with 0xff00, is not 0x2300, 0x2700 or 0x2800. Operation codes are the first operand of the `cache` instruction, in hex.
  A range no larger than `_sidcache_size` is handled in 32-byte lines when the count is at most 2048, with start rounded down and end rounded up to a line, and in 4096-byte pages with the same rounding when it is larger; an empty rounded range returns. It saves Status and TagLo, writes zero to both, and runs the tag store: operation 0xb per line (0xa after a `sync` when the implementation value is 0x2700) or operation 0x17 per page (0x16 after a `sync` for 0x2700), then restores TagLo and Status.
  A larger range goes to the whole-cache branch. When the word `_triton_use_invall` (0xa003c918, zero in the shipped image) is zero it saves Status and TagLo, zeroes both, runs the page operation 0x17 (0x16 with a `sync` for 0x2700) over `boot_sidcache_size` bytes from 0x80000000 in 4096-byte steps and restores them; when it is non-zero it issues one operation 0x3 at 0x80000000, or operation 0x2 for the implementation value 0x2700. The whole 736 bytes were read, including both tag-store loop pairs for the 0x2300 and 0x2700 implementations.
- `btrace` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the stack back-trace command, a 1144-byte routine at address 0xa0013938. Inferred.
  The arguments are the starting pc (a0), the stack pointer (a1), a return address to use for a first frame in a leaf routine (a2), the maximum number of frames (a3) and the lowest address to search (a4). When pc or sp is not word aligned it prints `pc:0x%x sp:0x%Ix must be word aligned` and returns -1. Otherwise it prints a header `SP FROM FUNC()` and, for each frame, searches backwards (at most 0x4000 bytes and not below a4) for the instruction that lowers sp (the word pattern 0x27bd0000, `addiu sp,sp,imm`, with a negative immediate; the helper at 0xa0013db0 does this search and a forward one for the return-address store, pattern 0xffbf0000, `sd ra,off(sp)`), reads the saved return address from the frame, checks that the word before it is a jump-and-link, prints the frame's sp, the caller symbol with its offset and the called function's symbol with up to the first arguments read from the frame (one search per entry of the two instruction tables at 0xa0037ee8 and 0xa0037f08), then moves sp up by the frame size and continues from the return address.
  It returns 0 after `--More--` when the frame limit is reached, 0 when it cannot find the sp adjustment (message `Can't find [D]ADDIU from 0x%x`) or the return address is zero or no store of it exists, and -1 when the return address does not follow a jump-and-link (`Ra:0x%x doesn't follow a jal?`). A debugging flag word (`bdeb`) adds `initial pc...`, `ra:... sp+raoff:...`, `Using passed ra`, `New sp...` and `Can't find STA?` lines.
- `_get` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the command that prints a memory location or a register, a 1044-byte routine at address 0xa0014c88. Inferred.
  The arguments are the argument count (a0) and vector (a1). One operand reads a word; with two the first may be `-b`, `-h`, `-w` or `-d` for a 1-, 2-, 4- or 8-byte read, and any other option, or any other argument count, returns 1.
  The operand is first looked up as a register name (0xa00152e0). A register prints `name:` with its decimal and `%R` field-decoded forms; the Config register prints only the decoded form, the tag register prints in both primary and secondary tag formats (descriptor tables chosen by the PRId implementation, below 0x23 meaning the older tables), and on the older implementations the cache-error register prints the failing cache's name (`icache`, `dcache`, `scache`, `sysad`) with that cache's descriptor table; a register without a descriptor prints decimal, hex and the character form. Otherwise the operand is an address: a symbol is tried first (`parse_sym`), then a number (`atob_L`); failure calls `_dbg_error` with `Illegal address: %s`. The width is narrowed by the low address bits (odd gives bytes; bit 1 set narrows words and doublewords to halfwords; bit 2 set narrows doublewords to words). An address above 0xbfffffff and below 0xff600000 that `probe_tlb` reports unmapped is mapped with `map_k2seg_addr`. The value is read by `_get_memory` and printed as `0x%08x: decimal 0xhex 'c'`.
  It returns 0 after printing and 1 for a bad option or operand count.
- `Open` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the firmware-style file-open entry of the debugger's own I/O library, a 916-byte routine at address 0xa0024e50. Inferred.

A null path returns 7 (the invalid-argument code of the ARCS error numbering). It takes an I/O record from `new_eiob` (none left returns 10, the too-many-open-files code), clears two of its words, and parses the path into it with `parse_path` (a non-zero result is returned after freeing the record). It rejects a console-protocol request on a component that cannot be a console (7) and a component without a strategy routine or marked failed (13), then maps the mode to flag bits in the record's flag word: read (bit 0), write (bit 1), create (0x100), supersede (0x200), directory (0x400) and the combinations of mode 0 to 8; if the component lacks the matching input or output capability the result is 2 (access refused); a mode above 8 returns 7. Opening a bare device in a directory, create or supersede mode returns 18 (not a directory). For certain component types it adds block and file-system flags (0x60 for the disk, tape, CD, WORM and floppy classes, 0x40 for the two network classes, 0x80 for the pointer class). It then calls the device strategy routine with the open function code; a failure returns the record's error number, or 8 when it is zero. With a file name given it searches the file systems (`fs_search`, error 20 when none matches) and calls the file system's open function. On success it stores the descriptor from the record in a2 and returns 0; on every failure the record is freed.

A `:` ends the expression, taking the following term as an end address and storing `(end - start) / unit` in the count place when there is one; a `#` ends it and stores the following term as the count itself. With the word zero it saves TagLo, clears it, sets the end address to 0x80000000 plus the size, and issues a page-invalidate on 4 KiB steps (`cache` operation 0x16 for part 0x27, 0x17 for the others, from -4096 relative to the advancing pointer; the loop ends when the pointer equals the end) after a `sync`, then restores TagLo. Otherwise it stores count minus 1 back (0xa002995c) and uses the slot at 64 bytes times the new count: it copies 16 words to 0x80000000 from slot offset +8, to 0x80000180 from +264, to 0x80000080 from +520 and to 0x80000100 from +776, in that order, then calls `__cache_wb_inval` (0xa0022ba0) on 0x80000000 with 448 bytes.

- `_ttyinput` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: takes one received character for a serial device record, applying flow control, the interrupt character and the debugger escapes before queueing it, a 312-byte routine at address 0xa00282a4. Established.
  The arguments are the device record (a0, kept in s1) and the character (a1,
  in s0, tested whole and masked to a byte in s2). The record's flags word (+0) bit 2 selects true raw mode: the character then goes straight to `_circ_putc` (0xa002846c) with the character and the record (0xa0028384). Otherwise:
  0x13 (^S) returns after setting flags bit 1 (stopped), unless `ignore_xoff` (0xa0058da0) is non-zero, in which case it returns with nothing changed (0xa00283a4). For any other character it clears flags bit 1 (the store at 0xa00282ec is in the delay slot of the ^Q test, so it also happens for ^Q), and 0x11 (^Q) returns. It then calls the function whose pointer is the word at 0xa0058d9c with the character; a non-zero result returns (the branch-likely `bnezl` at 0xa0028300 reloads `s0` in its delay slot only when taken).

Otherwise 0x04 (^D) calls `EnterInteractiveMode` (0xa0031758) and continues; 0x01 (^A) with the word at 0xa0001010 non-zero and the word `symmon` (0xa00391c0) zero calls the routine at 0xa002d2e0 with the string `ring` (0xa00375b0) and returns.

- `ioctl` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the control entry of the debugger's I/O library, which handles five requests itself and sends the rest to the device's strategy routine, a 312-byte routine at address 0xa0026ba0. Established.

  kept in s0) and the argument (a2, s1). It calls `get_eiob` (0xa0026e48); no record, or a record whose flags word (+44) is zero, returns -1. Requests, by value, with every case returning 0 unless stated: 0x6601 sets flags bit 2 (non-blocking) when the argument is non-zero and clears it otherwise;

  0xa002ca00, 0xa002ca28,

- `new_str2` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: adds a `name=value` string to a string list, a 308-byte routine at address 0xa002b6d4. Established.
  The arguments are the name (a0, kept in s2), the value (a1,

- `_string` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: the debugger's `string` command, which prints the zero-terminated text at an address in quotes, a 300-byte routine at address 0xa0015388. Established.
  The arguments are the argument count (a0, kept in s1) and the vector (a1,
  s0); the word at 0xa00390d8 in the command table holds its address, between the name `string` (0xa00358d8) and the usage text (0xa00358e0). A count below 2 or above 3 returns 1. The stack holds the maximum length at 0(sp), preset to 70 (the `li` at 0xa0015388), and the address as a doubleword at 8(sp). It calls `parse_sym_range` (0xa001c428) on word 1 with those two slots and width 1;
  a non-zero result calls `atob_L` (0xa002c0d8) on word 1 and, when the text it returns does not end, `_dbg_error` (0xa0018428) with `illegal address: %s`. With a count of 3 it converts word 2 into the length with `atob` (0xa002bea0), reporting `illegal maxlen:
  %s` the same way.

  The loop ends with `puts` of the quote and newline (0xa0032850), then returns 0. No `jal` in the image targets it.
  the address converter there is `atob_s`, here `atob_L`.
- `cpu_set_tod` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: writes an ARCS time record into the battery-backed clock, a 300-byte routine at address 0xa0029f94. Established.
  The argument is the time record (a0, kept in s0), whose halfword fields are year +0, month +2, day +4, hour +6, minutes +8, seconds +10. It returns whatever the clock-access routine returns. It first forms the seconds into the month: seconds plus 60 times minutes plus 3600 times hours plus 86400 times the day, less 86400 (the constant 0xfffeae80 added at 0xa002a010), the multiplications done by shifts and adds; the result is kept at 8(sp). It then fills an 8-byte buffer at 0(sp) with the packed-decimal routine at 0xa002a484 (a value of 100 or more gives 0x99): seconds, minutes, hours, day, month at bytes 0 to 4, the year modulo 100 at byte 5 and the year divided by 100 at byte 6, each with a signed divide. Byte 7 is the packed form of `_rtodc` (0xa002a218) on the seconds into the month, the year and the month, and is stored in the delay slot of the final call.
  The final call is the clock-access routine at 0xa002a4b4 with 1 (write) and the buffer. No `jal` or table word targets it.
- `ds2502_get_eaddr` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: reads the machine's 6-byte Ethernet address from the add-only memory chip, a 300-byte routine at address 0xa0028de4. Established.
  The argument is the 6-byte destination (a0, kept in s1); nothing is returned. It runs `ds2502_init` (0xa0028818); a zero result fills the destination with 0xff, written at +5 down to +0 (the value is loaded in the delay slot at 0xa0028df8), and returns. Otherwise it calls `ds2502_read_rom` (0xa0028a0c) with a 24-byte stack buffer and a count of 6; zero fills with 0xff. Then it calls `ds2502_read_ram` (0xa0028b4c) for 24 bytes from offset 0 into the same buffer; zero fills with 0xff. It then walks the buffer in three 8-byte blocks and calls `check_crc16` (0xa0028d24) on each.
  A zero result (the `beqzl` at 0xa0028e40 loads in its delay slot only when taken) copies block bytes 5, 4,
  3, 2, 1,
  0 to destination bytes 0 to 5 and returns; a non-zero result writes 0xff to all six destination bytes and tries the next block. After the third failure the destination is all 0xff.
  The `jal` sites that reach it are 0xa002732c and 0xa0027390.
- `flash_getversion` in `sbin/stand/symmon.IP32`: walks the segments of the flash ROM to find the one named `version` and returns the address of the version text in it, a 300-byte routine at address 0xa0028058. Established.
  It takes no argument. It returns the address of the version string, 8 bytes past the marker `VSEGOK`. It starts the walk with `nextSegment` (0xa0029ddc) on the flash environment record `envFlash` (0xa003c0e0, whose first word is 0xbfc00000) and a null previous segment,
  and repeats with the segment just returned (calls at 0xa0028074 and 0xa00280a8) until `nextSegment` returns zero.
  Each segment is printed with `printf` (0xa002e620) as `Current segment = %s` using the name at segment +20 (string 0xa00374d8).
  The name is compared with `version` (0xa00374f0) by `strcmp` (0xa0030938); a different name goes on to the next segment.
  For the `version` segment it prints `the version segment address = 0x%0x` with the segment address (0xa00374f8) and `the version body    address = 0x%0x` with segment +64 (0xa0037520), then scans 1024 bytes from segment +64, one byte at a time,
  comparing the text at each byte with `VSEGOK` (0xa0037548) by `strcmp`.
  A match prints `Found VSEGOK at location 0x%0x` with the address +8 (0xa0037550) and returns that address (the move into `v0` is at 0xa0028148). With no match in the 1024 bytes it calls `__assert` (0xa0027fc8) with the text `IMPOSSIBLE`, the file name `IP32.c` and the line 1205, then goes on with the next segment.
  When `nextSegment` returns zero it calls `__assert` with the same text and file and the line 1208; if `__assert` returns, the value returned is 0xa0037470, the address of the file-name string. No `jal` in the image targets it.
